BOTH of its stated grounds do not** --- premise 12's text was struck two days after that case was written, and PQ-10's serde hazard closed 2026-09-11 as *unreachable*. **Read that amendment before re-citing either ground.** **--- CORRECTION, same day: the PN-22 sentence above is SUPERSEDED and the row is no longer Open.** *Cost it against the corpus* was struck by the author as the wrong instrument --- **the corpus is adversarially sampled for falsifying power, never for typicality, so it cannot answer a frequency question** --- and the row's premise turned out weaker still: **there is NO DESCENT IMPLEMENTED ANYWHERE**, so presence today is neither a tree nor a DAG, and [Slice 7](#recommended-order) is a representation migration that adds none. **The obligation is GRADUATED to [`AGENT.implementation.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.implementation.md)'s `ludicGraph/` section and the row is retained only as its forwarding address.** **What stays true and is the whole of what this plan owes: [PN-20](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s `{ host, presence }` entry and [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s *one or more* quantifier keep the later choice free at zero cost --- do not let either regress.**

**This is a standard implementation plan under [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md), not a design-stage plan.** The deliverable is a migration --- types, a cut, a merge, a write side and a doc sweep --- and the open rows below are forks being resolved **in order to implement** it. **It is not a design plan even though it reopens design rows**, because it is not choosing between rival schemes: the scheme is chosen, and what the rows record is what the scheme costs where it lands on settled ground. If a genuine rival appears, that belongs in a proposal in [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md), not here.

**Scope in one sentence: make a presence binding a *node* of the graph rather than an index carried by a port, keep it addressable from outside the host as if it were still a port, and re-base `ludicCache`'s completeness bookkeeping on the node instead of on a field.**

## Getting Started

**Read the sections named below, not the files they are in.** The design plans in this directory run 200--1800 lines each and their decision rows are single lines of 5--20 KB. **Grep by row id** (`PR-15`, `PR-9`, `D3`, `H3`).

1. **[`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md)** once, for durability and content split. This plan is deleted at [Slice 8](#recommended-order); anything worth keeping moves to [`positions/AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md) or [`AGENT.implementation.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.implementation.md) first.
2. **[The proposal below](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses)** --- it is short and it is the whole subject. Read it before anything else in this list, so the rest reads as *what this costs* rather than as background.
3. **[`AGENT.edges.md`](../../../../../packages/mtw-wml/ts/standardize/keys/edges/AGENT.edges.md), the two-identity-schemes paragraph under [List typing note](../../../../../packages/mtw-wml/ts/standardize/keys/edges/AGENT.edges.md#list-typing-note).** **Chaining is a *port* mechanism: the legs of one crossing share an `edgeId` and are minted as a pair by a write path that already knows the relationship spans a boundary.** `Present`-kind cover edges are a **deliberately unrelated identity scheme** and carry no `edgeId` at all. **Read it before the presence rows below, not after.** The whole terminal-versus-crossing vocabulary PR-15 is denominated in is **port-level**, and a reader who reaches PR-15 without this re-asks the crossing question about presence *nodes*, where it is **undefined rather than hard** --- **that misreading cost a full conversation on 2026-09-15 and is why [PN-3](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is restated as it is.** Reading this paragraph first is the established habit, not a new one: [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#getting-started)'s Getting Started already does the same.
4. **[`AGENT.presence.planning.md`](AGENT.presence.planning.md), four rows and one section.** The [cover formulation](AGENT.presence.planning.md#presence-as-a-cover) --- **specifically its distinction between a *binding* (the semantics) and a *port* (the mechanism), which is what makes this migration cheap at the presence layer**; and from the [Settled register](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register), **PR-4** (`Present` edges run PORT -> NODE), **PR-9** (the cover ranges over nodes only), **PR-10** (implicit at arity <= 1) and **PR-15** (~~a presence port is a terminal, never a crossing~~ **--- read PR-15 for its *denotation* verdict, amended 2026-09-15: an edge landing on a presence binding denotes the part of the object present via that binding, the binding being the referent itself. Its terminal-only half is port-level and goes moot here, which is not the same as being moved --- [PN-3](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)**). ~~**Three of those four are moved by this plan and one is not**~~ **--- the count was always loose and the 2026-09-15 correction makes it misleading; read [what this plan disturbs](#what-this-plan-disturbs-and-what-it-leaves-alone) for the per-row disposition instead of trusting a tally.**
5. **[`ludicCache/types.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/types.ts)** end to end --- it is 120 lines and it is the structure being re-based. Note that `interiorConsolidated` ships as a **boolean** while [D3](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) has decided a per-binding **set** that is not yet built; this plan supersedes the shape and inherits the argument.
6. **[`ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts)** --- the cut. Two behaviours matter and neither is obvious from the type: **all** presence ports of the whole are carried into **every** bucket unconditionally (crossing ports are carried referenced-only), ~~and `nodesFromPresencePort` short-circuits to *the whole node list* when the graph carries one presence port or none~~ **--- STALE, corrected 2026-09-17+: [Slice 3](#recommended-order) DELETED that short-circuit ([PN-18](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)).** `nodesFromPresencePort` now looks up the presence NODE matching the id and branches on its `cover.tag` --- `'Full'` (or no node found) returns the component node list plus the root, `'Enumerated'` returns the cover's members plus the root. **The whole-node-list answer survives as the `'Full'` branch, so the behaviour is not gone; the arity test that used to reach it is.** Do not read the old sentence as describing live code, and do not restore an arity test on its authority --- PN-18 decided coverage is orthogonal to arity.
7. **[`ephemeraMeta.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts)**, the terminal and node blocks. `EphemeraLudicTerminalId` is a bare id **or** `{ owner, port }`; the parsed shape is the stored shape and **there is no string form in the domain layer** (PQ-9). `EphemeraLudicGraphNode`'s five tags each carry a `universalKey` that is a real component id --- [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is about the fact that a presence node has no such key.
8. **[`AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md`](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md)** --- **the in-flight sibling, and the coordination risk.** Read [the sequencing note](#relationship-to-the-rebuild-plan) below before touching either.

**Test orientation.** Command authority for this area is the parent design plan's [Verification](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#verification) section; if anything here conflicts with it, that wins. From the repo root:

```bash
cd lambda/ephemera && npm run test -- --watchAll=false dataSource/positions/
```

**`npm run test`, not `npm test`, and `npx tsc --noEmit` is not sufficient.** `*.integration.test.ts` files sit outside the tsconfig include and mock by module *path*, so a rename can typecheck clean and still break the suite. Run the baseline above before the first edit; it should pass.

## Why this is opened

**The trigger is a loss in `ludicCache`, stated by the author on 2026-09-14 and traced in the same conversation.** A cache is one merged graph over many wholes; a `ludicGraph` is many little graphs each with its own boundary. Presence in the graph is an **indexing of nodes by a boundary site**, and the merge has nowhere to put the site. Three losses follow, and they are the measure this plan is graded against:

- **(A) Enclosure of an otherwise unrelated node.** In a bucket graph the bucket *is* the node list; in a merge there is no node list to be.
- ~~**(B) Identity of the crossing site.** `chains: EphemeraMembershipHostId[][]` names **hosts**, so a string entering one room at two places collapses to one hop.~~ **--- RESTATED 2026-09-15, in conversation with the author, after reading the field's one producer. The sentence conflated two boundaries and misdescribed the mechanism twice.** **(1) Nothing collapses.** [mergeReducer.ts:177](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/mergeReducer.ts) concatenates entries on an identity-key match, and the test at `mergeReducer.test.ts`'s *groups two independently-matched leg pairs* pins the result as `[[boxId], [boxId]]` --- **two entries, side by side, deliberately unmerged, exactly as LR-8 requires.** The count of routes survives; what is lost is that the entries are **identical**, so they cannot be told apart. **Discriminability, not arity.** **(2) It is not "at the edge level."** The edge identity is intact; what is coarse is the route annotation, and the hop is minted **per crossing port** --- `collapseCrossingPorts` iterates `childGraph.ports.filter(isCrossingPort)`, `kind !== 'Present'` --- while writing `childGraph.hostId`, which is simply the only identity in scope at that line. **A presence node is nowhere on that path, and per [PN-3](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) a presence node is never a crossing at all**, so this loss as originally stated is not one presence nodes could recover. **Where the conflation came from, since it is an easy one to repeat:** a hop is typed `EphemeraMembershipHostId`, which is *membership* vocabulary, so hops read as though they recorded membership. They do not. **THE LOSS THAT IS REAL, and it is the one worth carrying:** the same child folded into one cache **more than once** --- present in `ROOM#A` and `ROOM#B`, both inside the merge --- produces entries that all read `[CHILD]`, because the hop names **the container descended into** and not **the binding descended through**. That is a membership fact, it is invisible at host grain, and it is what [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is actually about. **What is NOT a loss the cache needs to recover: which crossing port.** The cache is derived; the `ludicGraph`s remain the source of truth for crossings, and wanting port identity *in the cache* is wanting the cache to answer without consulting them. **This field has already been graded down once on exactly that reasoning** --- `chains` carried `EphemeraLudicCacheCrossing[][]` (`{ edgeText, into }`) until 2026-09-10, when per-crossing description was removed and only the host entered was kept ([`types.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/types.ts)). **Re-graining hops to crossing ports would walk that decision back.**
- **(C) Presence is not a node-to-node relation.** Encoding it as an edge requires the index itself to be a node --- which is this plan.

**The cache is *true* and meaningfully *incomplete*, and adding `Present` edges alone does not fix it** --- that was the author's opening claim and it survived the conversation. What fixes it is giving the boundary site an identity the merge can carry.

**The second motive is asymmetry, and it is why this is a *hybrid* rather than a straight reification.** Component nodes want to be localized by crossing ports, so their relations with other hosts are mediated. Presence nodes want the opposite: they need a reference back to *what they are presence for*, and they benefit disproportionately from being addressable **from outside** the host. Ports gave that addressing for free, which is why they were a tempting first implementation. The hybrid keeps it.

## The proposal in six clauses

**The author's, 2026-09-14. Recorded verbatim in substance; the commentary is separated below so the clauses can be read as the specification.** **--- the heading says *six* and there are now SEVEN: clause 5 is retired ([PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)) and clause 7 was added 2026-09-16.** **The heading is deliberately NOT renamed** --- `#the-proposal-in-six-clauses` is linked from this file and from amended rows in sibling plans, and cross-file anchors fail silently, so renaming it would break pointers to buy a corrected tally. **Read the list, never the heading's number** --- the same instruction [Getting Started](#getting-started) gives about this file's own summaries.

1. **A presence binding is a node.** For `ludicCache` it carries `consolidated: boolean` (undefined/false meaning not consolidated); it remains addressable from outside the host in port form, `OBJECT#CONTRAPTION#{presence node id}`. **--- EXTENDED 2026-09-16 by the course correction: the node ALSO carries its cover directly, `cover: EphemeraLudicGraphComponentNode['universalKey'][]`.** This is the clause the correction actually turns on --- *a presence node has its own ability to store relevant data* --- and the cover is the data most obviously its own. **Whether `cover` and `consolidated` remain two fields or collapse into one is [PN-15](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)**; do not assume either shape while that row is open. The exterior port-form address is **unchanged** and is not what the correction touches. **--- and the node may ALSO carry [clause 7](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses)'s `fullCoverage`, in which case it stores no member list at all.** **Whether that is a third field beside `cover` or an arm OF `cover` is [PN-19](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)**, which is why this clause deliberately does not spell the field shape out; **[PN-15](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) and PN-19 together decide how many fields this record actually has**, and answering either alone risks a record with three near-named per-binding facts on it.
2. **A host node may have NO presence sub-nodes linked to it**, which says its graph is **unexamined**.
3. **If a host consolidates *any* presence bucket, it adds *all* of its presence nodes** and connects them to the hosting node. Zero or all; never some.
4. **Each presence node carries `consolidated`** for its own bucket.
5. ~~**For a single-hosted node** --- exactly one presence node --- presence within that bucket is **implicit**, derivable from the hosting edges (`In`, `On`, `PartOf`).~~ **--- RETIRED 2026-09-15 by [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only). The hosting edges it derives from are not written: `drop` and `take-hold` mint none, and `In` is never minted at all.** Clause 5 pays for itself only if those edges exist for another reason; they do not, so deriving costs exactly what writing costs (one edge per node per bucket) and keeps an arity branch besides. **The clause is struck, not weakened --- there is no arity-1 special case anywhere in the design.**
6. ~~**For a multi-hosted node,**~~ **`Present` links are written explicitly** for consolidated buckets. **--- widened 2026-09-15 by [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), absorbing clause 5: *every* consolidated bucket gets explicit `Present` links, at mint time, regardless of arity.** ~~This is now the single rule for bucket membership, and the two clauses are one.~~ **--- RE-MECHANIZED 2026-09-16 by the course correction, and read the change precisely: what is struck is the `Present`-EDGE mechanism, not the rule.** The rule survives verbatim in content --- **every consolidated bucket states its members explicitly, at mint time, regardless of arity** --- and clause 5's retirement by [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) stands with it, since nothing is derived under either mechanism. **What changes is where the statement lives: clause 1's `cover` field on the presence node, instead of one `Present` edge per member.** **PN-1's verdict is NOT reopened by this** --- it weighed *deriving versus writing* and chose writing; the correction changes only the shape of the thing written, and on PN-1's own cost argument a field is strictly cheaper than N edges. **PN-1's standing prohibition also survives unchanged and is the one most at risk of being read as lapsed: do NOT materialize hosting edges (`In`/`On`/`PartOf`) as an alternative.** That is still rejected, now for a second reason as well --- the cover has a home on the record, so there is nothing an invented edge would be needed to express. **Clause 3 is NOT amended by any of this**, and the reason is worth stating so it is not "corrected" later: its *connects them to the hosting node* is the presence-node-to-HOST link, which Slice 2 already shipped as the `fromHostId` field on `EphemeraLudicGraphStructureNode` --- never a `Present` edge, which runs the other way (binding to MEMBER, [PR-4](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)). Clause 3's *zero or all* is untouched.
7. **`fullCoverage` --- ADDED 2026-09-16, the author's, in the same conversation as the course correction.** When present and true on a presence node, **that binding's cover is every node in the host, and no member list is stored.** **Legal in `ludicGraph`. ILLEGAL in `ludicCache`, where it must be expanded to an explicit cover list at the fold.** **The asymmetry is not a convenience rule; it is [loss (A)](#why-this-is-opened) restated as a legality.** *In a bucket graph the bucket **is** the node list; in a merge there is no node list to be* --- so **"every node in the host" has a referent in a single host's `ludicGraph` and NONE in a multi-host merged cache**, where it would silently mean *every node of every whole folded in*. **This clause is the shipped behaviour made explicit, not a new capability.** [`nodesFromPresencePort`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts)'s `presencePortCount <= 1` arm **already returns `new Set([...graph.nodeIds, root])`** --- full coverage, **derived from arity**. Clause 7 **stores the fact instead of deriving it**, which is precisely the derive-versus-write fork [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) already resolved in favour of writing, now applied one level down: **clause 6 made *which members* explicit; clause 7 makes *all of them* sayable compactly rather than by enumeration.** **What it does NOT interact with, recorded because the two read as if they should: clause 3's *zero or all*.** Clause 3 governs **which bindings exist** on a host; clause 7 governs **one binding's extent**. Orthogonal --- a host may be fully consolidated (clause 3) with one binding at full coverage and another enumerated. **Two rows: [PN-18](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)** (is `fullCoverage` restricted to arity <= 1, i.e. merely [PR-10](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) written down, or free at any arity and therefore a widening of it?) **and [PN-19](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)** (the encoding, and what makes the cache form unrepresentable rather than merely forbidden). **It also amends [PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)**, whose short-circuit half this answers with an option that row did not have. **--- [PN-18](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) DECIDED the same day, and it makes clause 7 stronger than first written: `fullCoverage` is ORTHOGONAL TO ARITY IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Any arity may carry it, **multiple full covers on one host are legal**, and a single-hosted node --- **full by construction, not by restriction** --- **writes the flag too rather than leaving it implicit.** **So clause 7 RETIRES the implicit form** that [PR-10](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) licensed, which goes **moot, not false**. **Read clause 7 as the single rule for a binding's extent, with no derived or implicit path beside it** --- the same shape clause 6 has for bucket membership, and the completion of [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s *no arity-1 special case anywhere*. **[PN-19](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) remains open on the encoding and is NOT pre-empted by this** --- *written at every arity* says the fact is always stated, not whether it is stated as a boolean beside `cover` or as an arm of it.

**Why clause 3 is the load-bearing one, and it should not be weakened for size.** It resolves a conflict between two things this design was otherwise holding at once. Carrying every presence port into every bucket (shipped) is what keeps **binding arity** readable from any bucket, which is what [D3](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s completeness claim rests on. The rule *a presence node is absent iff its bucket was not pulled* needs the opposite. **All-or-nothing per host gives both:** zero says *unexamined*, any says *the binding set is complete*, and the per-node flag says which were pulled. Neither scheme alone can say all three.

~~**Clause 3 also pays for clause 5, which answers the obvious objection to it.** An arity change (single-hosted becomes multi-hosted) flips a host from implicit to explicit and needs `Present` edges written retroactively for the first bucket --- which looks expensive until you notice that clause 3 **already** forces re-materializing all presence nodes for that host on any arity change. The conversion rides an operation that is whole-host anyway. **No row is opened for it; it is recorded here so it is not rediscovered as a cost.**~~ **--- MOOT 2026-09-15 with clause 5's retirement ([PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)). There is no implicit-to-explicit conversion left to pay for, because nothing is ever implicit.** Kept struck rather than deleted because the observation it rests on --- that clause 3 forces whole-host re-materialization on any arity change --- is still true and still worth knowing; what lapses is only the cost it was defraying.

**What the proposal banks, checked against the three losses:** ~~**(B)** and **(C)** are recovered --- two entries into one room are two nodes, and the index is a node by construction.~~ **--- amended 2026-09-15 with [(B)'s restatement above](#why-this-is-opened).** **(C) is recovered outright** --- the index is a node by construction, and nothing about that claim moves. **(B) is not recovered by the clauses at all, and it never was:** "two entries into one room are two nodes" described two **crossing** sites, which this migration does not reify and which [PN-3](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) puts outside a presence node's vocabulary entirely. **What the clauses do reach is (B)-as-restated** --- *which binding was descended through* --- and even that is **reachable rather than banked**, because it requires [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) to re-grain the hop and `collapseCrossingPorts` to be told which binding it is folding, which today it is not. **Read the plan's justification as resting on (A) and (C).** Both are independent of `chains`, both are what the six clauses actually build, and the plan stands unchanged if (B) is struck entirely --- which is the honest grading, and the reason this amendment does not disturb a single slice. ~~**(A)** is recovered *conditionally*, and the condition is [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only).~~ **--- amended 2026-09-15: (A) is recovered UNCONDITIONALLY.** [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) retired clause 5, so bucket membership is never derived and never conditional on a graph property --- every consolidated bucket states its members with explicit `Present` links. **The one thing that remains conditional is not (A) but materialization coverage** --- whether an intervening host was pulled at all --- which is [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s referential-integrity question, not a loss of the index. It also defeats **CC0's box-can-be-empty argument** against deriving consolidation state: an opened empty bucket is a presence node flagged `consolidated` with no members, distinguishable from no presence node at all, so the marker is the **node**, not its contents. **--- 2026-09-16, the course correction does not disturb this grading and the check is worth recording rather than re-running later.** (A) and (C) are recovered by a `cover` field **at least as directly as by edges** --- (C) because the index is still a node by construction, (A) because an otherwise-unrelated node is enclosed by being *listed*, which is the same recovery with one fewer indirection. (B)-as-restated is still **not** recovered by the clauses and still waits on [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)/Slice 4. **The grading rests on (A) and (C), exactly as before, so no slice moves on this account.** **The CC0 sentence above is the one line here that the correction genuinely puts in play**, because *a presence node flagged `consolidated` with no members* is precisely the shape [PN-15](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) asks about --- whether an empty cover and an unconsolidated bucket are told apart by a second field or by the cover's own presence. **Do not read this paragraph as having pre-answered PN-15**; it settled that the marker is the node, never which field carries it.

## What this plan disturbs, and what it leaves alone

**Cheap at the presence layer, and that is not luck.** [`AGENT.presence.planning.md`](AGENT.presence.planning.md) deliberately named the index a *presence binding* rather than a *port*, recording that *"a binding is realised as a **port** is PR-4's mechanism and stays swappable."* This plan swaps it. **PR-4 pre-answers the node-to-node objection** as well: `Present` edges run PORT -> NODE precisely so presence is not a relation between two components, and a presence node keeps that asymmetry rather than dissolving it.

~~**Three settled rows move, and Slice 0 owns reopening each in its home file rather than quietly working around it:**~~ **--- FOUR as of 2026-09-16, and the fourth ([PR-4](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register), added at the end of this list) is owned by [Slice 0b](#recommended-order) rather than Slice 0.** The principle is unchanged --- reopen in the home file, never work around quietly. **Read each row itself and not this list's gloss of it**, which is the standing instruction in [Getting Started](#getting-started) and which the 2026-09-15 corrections to two of these three bullets earned.

- **[PR-15](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)** --- ~~*a presence port is a terminal, never a crossing; no exterior edge refers to it in the crossing sense.*~~ ~~**Its premise is exactly what the hybrid removes.**~~ ~~Note that [PR-16](AGENT.presence.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) was split out of PR-15 on this same premise and is affected with it.~~ **--- amendment 2026-09-15: this bullet misread the row three ways, and correcting it splits PR-15 rather than moving it.** **(1) There is no no-exterior-edge premise to remove.** PR-15's verdict is *"YES --- a presence port **may** be a terminal for edges of any kind"*; foreign-kind edges landing there are **licensed**, and clause 1's exterior address continues that rather than departing from it. **(2) The row is two propositions with different fates.** The **denotation clause** --- an edge landing on a presence binding denotes *the part of the object present via that binding*, with the binding as the referent **itself**, "real and unmaterialized," never a pointer resolved by walking --- **transfers to a node unchanged, and is the half worth graduating.** The **terminal-only clause** --- never a crossing, the `kind === 'Present'` partition, kind-agreement as a category error --- **goes MOOT, not false**: **chaining is a port mechanism and happens across ports, never across nodes** ([`AGENT.edges.md`](../../../../../packages/mtw-wml/ts/standardize/keys/edges/AGENT.edges.md#list-typing-note)), so of a presence *node* the crossing question is **undefined rather than hard**. **(3) [PR-16](AGENT.presence.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) does NOT move with it** --- it asks whether *crossing* ports accept terminal edges, and **crossing ports are untouched by moving presence bindings off ports.** **What is actually disturbed is disposition, not doctrine:** the terminal-only clause has live consumers, and whether they stay live is [PN-8](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s question. See [PN-3](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only).
- **[PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)** --- *the cover is total and ranges over **nodes only**.* A presence node is now a node. See [PN-6](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only).
- **[PR-10](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)** --- *implicit at arity <= 1.* ~~**Not moved, and worth saying so:** clause 5 is PR-10 applied, not amended. What changes is only *where the implicit form is legible* --- it is exact in a structure that **is** one bucket and undefined in a **merge**, which is the whole reason clause 6 exists.~~ **--- amended 2026-09-15, checked against PR-10's own verdict cell rather than this file's gloss of it, and the struck claim was wrong: clause 5 was NEVER PR-10 applied.** **PR-10's verdict reads *"implicit for AT-MOST-ONE presence binding, and the test is **binding count, never host count**"*, and its rationale is *"the single bucket **is** the node list, and totality guarantees a separate record could never disagree with it."* **That is a different mechanism from clause 5's.** PR-10 derives nothing --- it observes that in a structure which *is* one bucket, the node list already states the answer. Clause 5 substituted a **derivation from the hosting edges (`In`/`On`/`PartOf`)**, which is not PR-10's mechanism and is the part that fails, because those edges are not written ([PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)). **So retiring clause 5 does NOT move PR-10, for a better reason than the struck text gave:** the two were never the same claim, and PR-10's own form survives intact --- it is exactly what [Slice 3](#recommended-order)'s `presencePortCount <= 1` bullet preserves. **What PR-10 *does* owe a note is a tension it already records itself:** its 2026-09-04 update says population *"now writes bindings explicitly"* and that its rationale *"now argues against something this plan is deliberately doing."* Writing `Present` at mint time for every bucket extends that same drift one step further. **It does not falsify the row, on the row's own wording --- *"implicit-is-permitted is not explicit-is-forbidden"*** --- so what is owed in [Slice 0](#recommended-order) is a pointer, not an amendment. **Grade this against PR-10's cell, not against this bullet, if it is ever re-argued.**

- **[PR-4](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) --- added 2026-09-16 with the course correction, and it is the only Settled row this plan RETIRES a clause of rather than re-mechanizing or clarifying.** *`Present` edges run PORT -> NODE.* **There is no such edge under the correction**, so unlike PR-9 (more exactly true) and PR-15 (split, half moot) this row loses a proposition outright. **It splits the way PR-15 split, and the split is the point:** the **asymmetry clause** --- presence is not a relation between two components, which is *why* the arrow ran port-to-node instead of node-to-node --- **transfers to the `cover` field unchanged and is the half worth graduating**, since an index owning a list of members is that same asymmetry as a record; the **edge clause** goes to [PN-14](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only). **Do not grade the verdict as false:** it was right about the mechanism it was written against, and note that **PR-4 was already load-bearing in the other direction** --- [the section above](#what-this-plan-disturbs-and-what-it-leaves-alone) cites it as pre-answering the node-to-node objection, and that citation survives the correction intact because it rests on the asymmetry half. **Slice 0b owns the amendment.**

**One Locked clause is the real cost, and it is one level up.** [H3 clause 1 / P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) --- *"ports are single-use; a port's exterior end is a graph plus an **optional** endpoint"* --- is **Locked**, and a presence node is not that shape. [PN-9](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is where this is raised; **it is raised, not decided here**, because a Locked clause is amended in its own file by its author.

**Several open rows get *easier*, and none of them should be closed opportunistically as a side effect of this work:** PR-3 (representation), PR-5 (query shapes), PR-12's cascade, PR-16, and the rebuild plan's [3d](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#recommended-order). Recorded so the improvement is visible; **closing a design row from an implementation plan is out of scope.**

## Relationship to the rebuild plan

[`AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md`](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md) is **in progress** and this plan overlaps it in three named places. **Read this before starting either.**

- **Rebuild [3c](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#recommended-order) / [D3](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is superseded in shape, not in argument.** D3 decided per-binding over a boolean and its reasoning stands entirely; what changes is that the base moves from a **field on the host node** to the **presence-node set** and the fiber to a flag on each. **D3 is Decided, so this is a reopen with an amendment, not a fill-in** --- [PN-7](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only). **Do not build 3c as written** if this plan is going ahead; building it first means building the field twice.
- **Rebuild 3d (referential integrity) is inherited, not duplicated.** Clause 3 makes *zero or all* a checkable invariant and an edge to an absent presence node a checkable reference, which is more structure than 3d had. [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only).
- **Rebuild [D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) (`chains`' name) and [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) (`chains`' grain) are one field and two questions.** ~~Decide them together or the field is edited twice.~~ **--- amended 2026-09-15: they were NOT decided together. [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) landed alone, and D8 is still open.** **They are genuinely separate questions** --- a rename does not re-grain and a re-grain does not rename --- which is why PN-2 is not simply folded into D8. **What the original caution was protecting is still protectable, because deciding and editing are different moments: nothing has touched the field yet.** **The rule that replaces it: do not WRITE the re-grain until D8 has answered.** Both verdicts then land in one edit, which is what *edited twice* was ever about. **And PN-2 did not leave D8 where it found it** --- the re-grain retires *route travelled* as the field's meaning in favour of *dependency held*, so `chains` now names something the field no longer does. **D8 acquired a live reason to move that it did not have when this note was written**, and should be read fresh rather than as the standing question it was. **--- D8 ANSWERED the same day, 2026-09-15: `chains` becomes `supportedBy: EphemeraLudicCacheSupport[]`, members UNORDERED**, the element type restored (its 2026-09-10 deletion is diagnosed there as why the two-level semantics went unwritten), and both of D8's own candidates rejected as *route-travelled* names. **So the sequencing rule is discharged on the deciding side and now binds only the writing: the rename and the re-grain are ONE edit, and neither is to be written alone.**

**Sequencing recommendation, not a rule:** this plan's Slices 0--2 (reopen, corpus, types) are independent of the rebuild's Slices 1--2 and can run alongside them. **Slices 3--5 here collide with rebuild Slice 3** and one of the two should land first; **this one, if the migration is going ahead at all**, since the rebuild's fold would otherwise be written against a structure this plan replaces.

## Recommended order

Pending work uses `[ ]`, completed work `[X]`; mark nested lines `[X]` as each sub-step finishes, so partial progress on a slice is visible. Updating these boxes is part of *done* for a slice, after tests pass.

- [ ] **Slice 0. Reopen what this migration moves. Documentation only, and genuinely blocking.** Every slice below is reached by a reader who will otherwise find a Settled row contradicting the code in front of them. **Amend, never rewrite in place** --- `~~struck~~` plus `**--- amendment**`, per [`taskPlanning/AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md).
  - [ ] **Field-name sweep, added 2026-09-15 with [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)/[D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)/[PN-7](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only). Two fields, and THREE live names for one of them.** **(1) The cache edge's route field** is called `crossings` in [`AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md)'s **CC1b** (its pre-2026-09-10 name), `chains` in the shipped type and across both implementation plans, and becomes **`supportedBy`** under D8. **A reader of the parent design plan is two renames behind**, which is exactly how a stale name gets re-adopted by someone working from the design plan rather than the code. **(2) `interiorConsolidated`** occurs in ~~**15 places across four planning files**~~ **--- RECOUNTED 2026-09-16: 26 occurrences on 19 lines**, across the same four planning files (rebuild 11 on 6 lines, this file 8 on 7, `AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md` 3 on 2, the ludicCache corpus 4 on 4) --- and is **deleted** by PN-7. **15 was a miscount, not a narrower scope**: the identifier is distinctive and occurs in no other sense anywhere in the corpus, so there is no reading of *places* that yields 15 and the smaller number would have stopped the sweep early. **Both numbers are given because *places* was the ambiguity** --- count LINES when working the sweep, since several lines carry the name twice. **Two of the 19 are self-referential and must not be "swept" into nonsense:** this item itself, and [PN-7](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), whose row is the record of the deletion and keeps the name by design. **Code sites are NOT in this count** --- the declaration and guard in `types.ts` plus its fixtures are PN-7's build task in [Slice 4](#recommended-order), and the counts move in opposite directions (the code sites go to zero; Under the bare-uuid encoding that distinction **was not expressible here at all**; the exterior address now carries the `PRESENCE#` tag, so it is decidable from the terminal alone via `isPresenceTaggedPortId`. **`resolveEndpoint` already models the split** (two distinct decline reasons where it previously had one conflated string) --- **read it as the precedent for this guard's two verdicts rather than re-deriving them.** Nothing of this slice is built.

## Open decisions (implementation --- plan only)

Plan-only: decisions being made in order to implement the slices above. When one ships, record it in `AGENT.contract.md` / `AGENT.implementation.md` and remove the row. **IDs are stable and never reused.**

| ID | Decision | Blocks slice | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **PN-1** | **What guarantees clause 5's derivation, and is it enforced?** Clause 5 says presence in a single bucket is derivable from hosting edges (`In`/`On`/`PartOf`). That requires every interior node to be **reachable from the host node by hosting edges, within the cache** --- including that the intervening nodes are themselves materialized. **A bucket graph never needed this property because it had a node list; a merge has none, which is loss (A) restated.** So clause 5 re-admits (A) in the arity-1 case exactly to the extent the property fails. **This is the same class of thing as *at most one leg of a chain per host*** --- true by construction today, checked nowhere, and load-bearing --- and that precedent says to write it down and consider a guard rather than rely on it silently. ~~**Decide: state it as an invariant with a check, state it as a documented construction property, or abandon clause 5 and always write `Present` edges.** The third is the safe answer and costs one edge per interior node per host, which is why it is not the default.~~ ~~[Slice 1](#recommended-order)'s unrelated-coin case is this row's falsifier.~~ **--- DECIDED 2026-09-15, in conversation with the author: the third option, and it is *dominant* rather than merely *safe*, which is a different verdict than the struck text offers. Clause 5 retires; `Present` links are written at mint time for every consolidated bucket regardless of arity.** **(1) The falsifier already fired, and not from the corpus.** The unrelated coin is not a case Slice 1 might or might not construct --- **it is the default state of the commonest thing in the game**, demonstrable from three shipped call sites in [`positions/index.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/index.ts): **drop** (-> room) and **take-hold** (-> character) pass no `containment` and so mint **no hosting edge at all**, while **rehost** (-> an object) is the only mobile route that states one. `containment?:` is optional at every level of the move stack (`orchestrateObjectMove`, `buildObjectMoveOp`, `planObjectMoveTransfer`, `positionKernelOp:90`), [`findOwnRootContainmentEdge`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/membership/findOwnRootContainmentEdge.ts) is written to return `undefined`, and `presencePortStepsForMove` runs **unconditionally** in `compilePositionKernelOp:133` independent of it. **So a loose object has no hosting edges to derive from, and clause 5 as written trades the only *total* record of bucket membership (the presence binding) for a *partial* one (a sparse annotation only `rehost` writes).** **(2) This row was two properties, and only one of them was ever this row's.** **(i) Per-shard:** every interior node has a hosting edge to its own shard root. That is **[AB-53](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#settled-register)**, graduated to [`AGENT.concepts.md:398`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md) --- *"within any one graph, every containment edge is incident to the root, so the containment subgraph is a **star**"* --- already documented in exactly the constructor-discipline form this row's second option proposed, with a named re-open trigger and a named list of what rests on it. **But AB-53 states a property of *edges* (every containment edge touches the root), NOT of *nodes* (every node has a containment edge), and clause 5 needs the second.** AB-53 is also scoped explicitly away from here: *"`ludicCache` nests more deeply by design and is not bound by that discipline."* **(ii) Cross-shard:** every intervening host between a node and the bucket it is attributed to is itself materialized. **That half survives this decision unchanged and forwards to [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)**, where absence-as-signal versus absence-as-corruption is already the question; it is not re-opened here and no new row is owed for it. **(3) The economics, which is what actually decides it.** Clause 5 pays for itself **only if the hosting edges are already there for another reason** --- the whole point of deriving is to read structure someone else wrote. Nobody writes it. So the live alternatives were: **materialize `In` at merge time** (per [PN-13](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s reading), or **write `Present` at mint time**. **These cost exactly the same --- one edge per node per bucket, not approximately** --- which means materializing `In` pays clause 6's price to obtain clause 5's mechanism, and still keeps the arity branch, the derivation rule and this row. **Equal price, one strictly fewer mechanism: that is dominance, not a tradeoff.** **(4) What the decision buys structurally: clauses 5 and 6 collapse into one rule** --- *every consolidated bucket has explicit `Present` links* --- with no arity branch anywhere in the design. **[Clause 57's](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses) arity-change conversion goes moot**, since nothing converts. **(5) Two asymmetries recorded because they would decide it even at unequal cost.** **Fabrication:** a merge-time `In` edge is data in the **source's** vocabulary that no source contains --- the cache would hold hosting edges no `ludicGraph` ever authored, which matters given `ludicCache` is headed for prompt construction. `Present` is the **cache's own** vocabulary, which clause 6 writes for the multi-hosted case regardless, so arity 1 adds no new *kind* of claim. **One-way door:** PN-13 keeps *unstated is `In`* as a **reading**, which costs nothing precisely because it stays semantic; materializing makes it structural and *author said `In`* becomes permanently indistinguishable from *merge inferred `In`*. **--- RE-MECHANIZED 2026-09-16 by the course correction; the VERDICT is not reopened.** This row weighed **deriving versus writing** and chose writing; the correction changes only **the shape of what is written** --- the presence node's `cover` field instead of one `Present` edge per member --- and on this row's own cost argument **a field is strictly cheaper than N edges, so the verdict wins by a wider margin than when it was taken.** **Clause 5 stays retired and there is still no arity special case anywhere.** **The prohibition in this row's rationale survives verbatim and is the part most likely to be misread as lapsed: do NOT materialize hosting edges (`In`/`On`/`PartOf`) in the cache as an alternative** --- the argument quoted just above (that materializing makes a semantic fact structural, and *author said `In`* becomes indistinguishable from *merge inferred `In`*) is **untouched by the correction**, and now has a second reason behind it: the cover has a home on the record, so no invented edge is needed to express it. | 1, 4 | **Decided 2026-09-15 --- clause 5 retires; ~~`Present` written~~ **the `cover` field written (re-mechanized 2026-09-16)** at mint time for every consolidated bucket** |
| **PN-2** | **Does `chains` re-grain to name presence nodes?** ~~`chains: EphemeraMembershipHostId[][]` names **hosts**, which is precisely why a string entering one room twice collapses --- loss (B) at the edge level. Presence nodes make the crossing site nameable, **so the node-level gain is not actually banked unless the hops name presence nodes rather than hosts.**~~ **--- PREMISE REPLACED 2026-09-15, in conversation with the author, against the field's one producer rather than against this row's gloss of it. The verdict is not pre-empted; the question is.** **What was wrong:** the row argued from a *representational loss* (a crossing site that cannot be named) and prescribed a *membership* cure (presence nodes), which are two different boundaries --- see [(B)'s restatement](#why-this-is-opened) for the mechanism. A hop is minted per **crossing port** and a presence node is never a crossing ([PN-3](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)); and the author's own test settles the half that remains: **would `ludicCache` ever care which specific port was used?** No --- **that would be wanting the cache to answer without consulting the graphs, which is treating a derived cache as an alternate source of truth.** The field was already graded down on that reasoning once (`edgeText`, 2026-09-10). **THE QUESTION THIS ROW NOW ASKS, and it is a smaller one: should a hop name the *unit of consolidation* rather than the container descended into?** **The argument is symmetry with a decided row, not a new claim.** [D3](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) re-grained `interiorConsolidated` from a node boolean to a **per-binding set** on the ground that *"only the second makes incremental rebuild viable"* --- so the cache's **node-side** bookkeeping already answers *which of my buckets have been pulled* at binding grain, while `chains`, the **edge-side** half of the same question (*by which fold did this edge arrive*), still answers at host grain. **Two bookkeeping fields, one notion of a unit, two grains.** **The concrete case, which host grain cannot express:** one child folded into one cache twice --- present in `ROOM#A` and `ROOM#B`, both inside the merge --- yields entries that all read `[CHILD]`, so re-pulling or dropping one binding cannot identify the edges that arrived through it without discarding everything that arrived through that child. **This is cache bookkeeping, not representation**, which is why it is at home in a derived cache and needs no appeal to loss. **AND IT IS NOT FREE, which is the whole of what is left to weigh.** **(1) A signature change, not a field edit:** `collapseCrossingPorts(parentGraph, childGraph)` consults no presence ports and is never told which binding it is folding --- supplying one changes what the caller owes the reducer. **(2) The hop stops being globally resolvable:** `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is resolvable on its own, a presence node id is **host-local** ([PN-11](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), settled against this plan's own clause 1), so a hop becomes the qualified exterior form `OBJECT#CONTRAPTION#{id}` --- changing the element type, `isEphemeraLudicCacheEdge`'s guard, and the shared-type note at [`types.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/types.ts)'s head. **Neither is an objection; both are work that must be named before this is called cheap.** **One caution against a tempting shortcut:** `chains` has **no reader anywhere** outside its own type guard and its own accumulator, and multi-hop chains have **no producer at all** (the only two-hop value in the repo is a type-guard fixture; chain accumulation across a membership boundary is [D1](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s explicitly-deferred Slice 3 generalization). **Do not read that emptiness as either licence or verdict** --- a prototype field with no consumers is as likely unfinished as unnecessary, and [`AGENT.md`](../../../../AGENT.md)'s standing caution against arguing from present state to design standing applies with full force here. **Decide with the rebuild plan's [D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)** (whether the field keeps its name, which collides with P8's *chain*): one field, two independent questions, and editing it twice is the avoidable outcome. **Note what does not follow:** re-graining does not by itself make a cache edge chain-shaped --- that is [D9](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) and stays there. **And a second thing that does not follow, recorded 2026-09-15 because it sits close enough to be re-litigated otherwise: a presence-node hop does NOT fire [PN-11](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s re-open trigger.** That trigger is *a presence node becomes **a terminal of a chain***, and a hop is neither terminal nor identity --- it is route bookkeeping in the `from`/`to`-independent `chains` list, exactly the *route provenance, not identity semantics* half of the same distinction PN-11 drew when it closed. **The trigger fires if a presence node appears in a cache edge's `from` or `to`, or if `chains` is given P8's identity semantics; naming one in a hop is neither.** **WHAT THE FIELD IS *FOR*, established in conversation with the author 2026-09-15 and the thing that makes the rest of this row gradeable --- it had never been written down.** A chain entry is **derived and re-derivable**: it records that a parent leg and a child leg were found and collapsed, a real structural fact about two `ludicGraph`s. **But the hop is not a fact about the edge --- it is a record of the descent the materializer took**, and nothing in an edge's own legs names a binding (the parent leg terminates at `{ owner: childId, port: crossingPortId }`, a **crossing** port, never a presence one). **So when a child holds two bindings into one parent there is no fact of the matter in the graphs about which one its crossing edges belong to; only the walk knows, and only the walk can say.** That is exactly why this is a caller-contract change and not a lookup. **And the purpose the bookkeeping serves is DEMATERIALIZATION, stated by the author:** when a presence bucket is dropped from a cache, (a) discard the chains that depended on that bucket, then (b) discard any edge left holding **none**. **The field is a reference count over materialization routes** --- which retroactively explains [LR-8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s insistence that entries are never merged or tie-broken: **merging two routes corrupts the count**, and what looked like fastidiousness about representation is load-bearing for a drop path. **THE HOLE THIS EXPOSES, and it is the strongest argument in this row.** `chains` is *"required and possibly empty, never optional"* --- and **both** same-host reconstruction paths write it: `collapseSameHostStubs`, whose doc comment states the ground --- *"`chains` is always `[]` --- this never crosses a membership boundary, so there is no hop to record"* --- and `foldSameHostBuckets`, which writes `chains: []` for every interior edge its fold resolves. **True of host boundaries, and misleading about dependency.** Those edges straddle two of the host's **own** presence buckets, and `foldSameHostBuckets` proves the dependency itself: pull one bucket only, and the straddling edge stays bound to an unresolved stub and is, in its own words, **"correctly not emitted."** **It is in the cache only because both buckets were materialized, and it records neither** --- so under the drop rule it either never loses a chain (a stale edge surviving the dematerialization of a bucket it depended on) or reads as zero and is dropped at once. **Both wrong, and the defect is that `empty` currently means two incompatible things: *no dependency* and *dependency not expressible*.** **THE RE-GRAIN CLOSES THIS FROM BOTH SIDES AT ONCE, which is the author's reading and the reason to prefer it:** it supplies a bookkeeping vocabulary for hops, **and in that vocabulary the same-host case acquires an entry it never had** --- a straddle has no host to name but exactly two **bindings** to name, so host grain here is not coarse, it is **empty**. **The definition that results is uniform and total: a hop names a binding this edge's presence in the cache depends on**, replacing *"the hosts entered"* --- and the reference count becomes complete rather than complete-except-for-a-class. **Feeds [D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) directly (the field's *name*, decided jointly per the note above): under this definition `chains` is actively misleading**, since the field would no longer describe routes travelled but dependencies held, and the *chain* overload with `chainId` and with P8 is already this row's sharpest hazard. **NOT decided here** --- D8 is the rename and this is the grain, and the two stay separate exactly as the note above requires. **DECIDED 2026-09-15, with the author: YES, re-grain --- a hop names a BINDING, not a host.** **The definition, and it replaces *"an ordered list of the hosts entered"* wholesale: a hop names a binding that this edge's presence in the cache DEPENDS ON.** Route-travelled is retired as the field's meaning; dependency-held replaces it, and the two coincide everywhere the old definition was not simply silent. **Why this is dominance rather than preference, and it is worth being exact because the row spent most of its life arguing the weaker case:** the deciding argument is **not** that host grain is coarse. It is that for a same-host straddling edge host grain **cannot state the dependency at all** --- there is no host to name and exactly two bindings to name, so the record is not imprecise but **empty**, and no amount of care at host grain recovers it. **A grain that can express a dependency beats one that cannot; there is no axis on which the old grain wins**, which is why this is settled without a measurement. **The fixture in [Slice 1](#recommended-order) is therefore a demonstration and a regression guard, no longer a decider.** **WHAT THIS COMMITS, all of it named so none of it arrives as a surprise:** **(1)** `collapseCrossingPorts` must be **told which binding it is folding** --- a caller-contract change, since it consults no presence ports today and the fact is recoverable only from the walk; ~~**(2)** the same-host paths (`collapseSameHostStubs`, `foldSameHostBuckets`) **stop writing `chains: []` for straddling edges** and write the bindings they span, which is the entry that did not previously exist --- `[]` narrows to its one honest meaning, *depends on no binding*;~~ **--- (2) SUPERSEDED 2026-09-16 by [D11](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only). The same-host paths KEEP writing `[]`, and it is CORRECT.** This clause was written while `chains` was the only register a dependency could go in. D11 splits the registers: a straddling edge's dependence on its two buckets is a **membership** fact about its ENDPOINTS, and membership now lives on the cache **node** record. The traversal register is then honestly empty --- a same-host edge crosses no membership boundary --- so same-host edges are **exactly the population `[]` exists to describe**, not an exception to it. The drop still bites, from the other register: lose a bucket, the terminals it justified lose their justification, and the edges touching them are culled. **(2') THE NARROWER OBLIGATION THAT REPLACES IT:** `foldSameHostBuckets` cuts each bucket with `nodesFromPresencePort(graph, portId)` --- *these nodes are justified by this binding* --- and **discards that fact today**. It must instead populate the node-side endpoint register. **The fact is in hand; the gap is that nothing carries it to `cache.nodes`**, and because the fold accumulates every bucket into one graph before reading edges off wholesale, this is a data-flow change through the accumulator rather than a field edit. **And `collapseSameHostStubs` is discharged outright** --- it produces only edges, and edges no longer carry endpoint facts, so the signature change once thought parallel to clause (1) is not needed. **Clause (1) stands unchanged:** traversal hops still require `collapseCrossingPorts` to be told which binding it is folding; **(3)** the hop's element type stops being `EphemeraMembershipHostId` and becomes the qualified exterior form of clause 1 (`OBJECT#CONTRAPTION#{presence node id}`), because a presence node id is **host-local** ([PN-11](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)) --- carrying `isEphemeraLudicCacheEdge`'s guard and the shared-type note at [`types.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/types.ts)'s head with it. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DECIDE, unchanged by the verdict:** the field's **name** ([D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), now with a live reason to move --- *chains* describes the retired meaning); whether a cache edge becomes **chain-shaped** ([D9](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), and the firewall above **still holds and still must survive**); and nothing about **P8** identity semantics, per the trigger note above. **AMENDED 2026-09-16, with the author. The re-grain stands unchanged --- a hop names a binding --- and the SHAPE the hops sit in gains a level. Written in [D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) rather than here, per this row's own joint-edit rule; read it before implementing clause (1) or (3) of WHAT THIS COMMITS.** In short: a support stops being a flat set of bindings and becomes a **set of hops, each hop a set of the bindings that could support it**, because the flat form is DNF and a route of *h* hops with *k* alternatives each expands to *k^h* entries carrying *k*h* facts. Three quantifiers --- OR over routes, AND over hops, OR within a hop --- which is also what separates the multiplicity of **routes** from the multiplicity of **support**, previously carried by one list and therefore multiplied. **TWO THINGS IN THIS ROW ARE DIRECTLY TOUCHED.** **(a) The port question, retired above, is RE-OPENED and provisionally reversed.** This row struck it on the author's test --- *"would `ludicCache` ever care which specific port was used? No"* --- and **that answer was correct for the purpose this row weighed, which was dematerialization alone.** A hop now carries `port` for a different job: it is the **hop's key**, distinguishing *two bindings supporting one hop* from *two hops*, and it is the only thing that lets a partial edge terminating at `{ owner: HOST, port: P }` be matched to a longer edge whose collapse erased P from its terminal. That is **consolidation**, a purpose the field did not have when this row answered. The finding is a DISTINCTION, and without it candidate three looks catastrophic.** **Bucket *membership* and survival of the *cut* are two different things, and [`subGraphFromNodes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) already separates them --- for presence ports, explicitly and on purpose.** Its contract: the returned graph's `ports` are the minted stubs **plus "all of `graph`'s own presence ports, *regardless of bucket*" (LR-6)**, with a stated reason --- `fromHostId` is a whole-graph fact, so carrying every presence port into every cut *"makes that fact recoverable from any single bucket's sub-graph with no merge-time reconciliation needed later."* **Read naively, candidate three says a presence node is in no bucket, therefore not in the cut, therefore absent from the materialized cache --- which would leave [PN-7](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s per-bucket consolidation status and [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s `supportedBy` with nothing to hang on.** **That inference is wrong, and the port precedent is why:** a presence port is a member of no bucket today and appears in every cut anyway, because a **structural carry rule**, not membership, is what puts it there. **So under [PN-8](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s *both*, the LR-6 carry rule MOVES from `ports` to `nodes` when the port reifies** --- same rule, same reason, same regardless-of-bucket scope. **What this row therefore decides is membership only, and candidate three is the answer**: a presence node is in no bucket, and is in every cut. **The two candidates it beats are not merely unargued now --- they are the ones that would make the carry rule redundant by *overloading membership to do its job*, which is exactly the conflation that made this row look hard.** **Candidate two is textually *"in every bucket"* and would look, from the outside, identical to the carry rule's effect** --- and that is the trap: it would state as a *cover fact* something that is really a *cut-construction fact*, so PR-9's totality would then be claiming something about the index rather than about the indexed. **Write both halves or neither** --- membership answered here, carry rule as a Slice 4 task --- **because half of this is worse than none: candidate three without the carry rule genuinely does delete presence nodes from every bucket.** **(4) `Totality is maintained by construction` does not settle this, and the constructor currently implements *two different answers*.** [`nodesFromPresencePort`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) has two arms: at **zero or one** presence port it returns `new Set([...graph.nodeIds, root])` --- *everything*, so if presence nodes reach `nodeIds` they land in every bucket by accident (candidate two, unchosen); at **more than one** it builds membership from the `to` endpoints of `Present` edges, which a presence node never is (candidate three, also unchosen). **Whichever candidate wins, one arm needs an edit** --- candidate two must add presence nodes to the multi-port arm, candidate three must filter them out of the degenerate arm. **PR-9's by-construction clause means the constructor *defines* the domain, not that it has already answered; it has not been asked.** Carry this as a Slice 4 cost attached to each candidate, equal in size either way, so it grades none of them --- **its force is that it removes `the code already does this` as an argument for any candidate.** **--- DECIDED 2026-09-15: CANDIDATE THREE. A presence node is a member of NO bucket, and appears in EVERY cut.** **The two clauses are one answer and neither ships without the other.** **On membership:** a presence node is the **index** of the cover, not a member of it --- PR-9's domain clause is restated over [`EphemeraLudicGraphComponentNode`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts) ([PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s partition), which is a **clarification of the domain term, not an amendment to a Settled verdict**: totality over the indexed set is exactly as total as PR-9 settled it, and the cover is now visibly a function *from* presence nodes *to* sets of component nodes. **On the cut:** the LR-6 carry rule moves from `ports` to `nodes` under [PN-8](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s *both* --- same rule, same reason (`fromHostId` is a whole-graph fact, recoverable from any single bucket with no merge-time reconciliation), same regardless-of-bucket scope. **Three considerations and no dissent, which is why this is decided rather than judged:** [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) retired clause 5 and with it candidate three's only recorded objection; [PN-13](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) clause 2 needs an explicit carve-out under candidates one and two and none under this one; [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) removed the amendment cost. **Candidates one and two are not merely unargued --- they are wrong in kind**, because each overloads *membership* to do the **carry rule's** job, stating as a cover fact what is a cut-construction fact. **Candidate two is the dangerous one precisely because it is observationally identical from outside** --- *"in every bucket"* and *"in every cut"* produce the same node lists, so nothing downstream would ever have caught it; 'qualified' : 'disqualified'` --- **a binary that has no correct answer here.** **Under this row's verdict (in no bucket) the status becomes `disqualified`, and an edge with one qualified end and one disqualified end MINTS A STUB CROSSING PORT** --- a spurious straddle, asserting a crossing where nothing crosses. **This is live, not hypothetical: [PR-15](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) settles that *"a presence port may be a terminal for edges of any kind"*, denoting the part of the object present via that port**, and the `Present`-kind carve-out (LR-6) does not cover these --- they are other kinds. **Under candidate two the status would be `qualified`, anchoring such an edge to every bucket, which is a different wrong answer, not a right one.** ~~**THE THIRD CLAUSE: a presence node is `neutral` in `endpointStatus` --- explicitly, by structure-node kind, never by owner comparison.** **It is the same answer the port has today and for the documented reason:** the neutral cases are those *"available in every bucket, and that is the only fact classification needs from either"* --- which is precisely what clause (b)'s carry rule makes true of a presence node.~~ **--- OVERTURNED the same day; see the CLAUSE (c) OVERTURNED amendment at the end of this row. state it as the split and they stop being a list of patches.** **The decisive evidence is that the shipped code ALREADY implements the two separately --- for the root --- and only the *vocabulary* conflates them.** [`subGraphFromNodes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) forces the root into `nodes` unconditionally (*"the root is in the bucket unconditionally (PR-9), regardless of which arm below applies"*), **and separately short-circuits it to `neutral` in `endpointStatus` by an `owner === root` test that runs BEFORE the `nodes.has` lookup.** **Being in `nodes` is not what makes the root neutral** --- the two behaviours are two pieces of code, and the membership insertion serves (1) alone. **The function's own doc comment has been saying this the whole time:** content edges are classified *"by **the status of each endpoint**, not by whether its owning component happens to land in `nodes` --- the difference is the whole of this rule"*, and the root and the host boundary are grouped because *"they have the same status. one.** **WHAT SURVIVES OF THE STRUCK CLAUSE, unchanged and still load-bearing:** (i) `nodes` is overloaded --- membership set *and* `endpointStatus` input --- and this row is where that surfaces; (ii)
