| **PN-9** | **[H3 clause 1 / P3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) is Locked and a presence node is not its shape.** The clause reads *"ports are single-use; a port's exterior end is a graph plus an **optional** endpoint."* **This is the real cost of the migration and it is one level up from this plan.** **This row raises it; it does not decide it** --- a Locked clause is amended in its own file by its author, and an implementation plan that quietly works around one is the momentum failure the lock exists to prevent. ~~**What to raise, specifically:** whether a presence node is a *port* under the clause at all (if not, the clause is untouched and the hybrid's port-shaped address is an addressing convention rather than a port), or whether it is a port whose exterior end is now a **node** rather than a graph-plus-optional-endpoint. **The first answer costs nothing and is probably right; do not assume it.**~~ **--- DECIDED 2026-09-15 BY THE AUTHOR, and the row's own first answer was right: a presence node is NOT a port. H3 CLAUSE 1 IS NOT AMENDED; P3 IS.** **The premise needed correcting first, and it was wrong twice.** **(1) The quoted formulation is not clause 1's.** Clause 1 reads, in full, *"Ports are the boundary mechanism. A whole owns its own interior graph with a root node; **crossing into or out of it happens only through numbered, single-use ports bound at the host boundary**"* --- **entirely about crossings**, silent on presence and on endpoints. **Presence bindings were never crossings ([PR-15](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)), so removing them from the port population leaves the clause untouched and in fact CLEANER: the port population becomes exactly the crossing mechanism, with no non-crossing tenant.** **(2) There is no optional-endpoint field, and P3 explicitly rejected one.** Its [default 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) reads *"A port with no exterior edge is a pure presence indicator... **better than an optional-endpoint field, because the absence of an edge IS the signal** --- no second record shape."* **The row raised a Locked clause against a shape that never shipped.** **Note the tier, since it decides who may amend what:** default 2 is one of P3's *"two defaults the shape enables"*, **not** one of the two shape claims clause 1 records as locked. **THE AMENDMENT, IN THREE ITEMS.** **(a) Retire P3's default 2.** Once presence bindings are nodes, absence-of-exterior-edge signals nothing, because nothing is left for it to signal --- **it loses its only case, and every other case was already closed**: a crossing port has *"exactly one interior edge and one exterior referrer"*; a partial release *"is not a state the model has anywhere else"* and release takes both legs together; and the broken-address case was split off from the atomic case on 2026-08-26. **(b) Tighten the conflict rule in [`AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md#port-records-field-scope-and-the-conflict-rule)**, whose normative form defers the exterior label to an exterior reference ***"when one exists to disagree with"*** --- **that conditional IS the optionality, and it can never be false once presence leaves the port population. A condition that cannot be false invites a branch nobody needs.** **(c) Fix the code contradiction, confirmed by the author 2026-09-15 as a contradiction rather than defensive reading:** [`classifyLudicGraphPortMismatch.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-gateways/ts/ephemera/positions/classifyLudicGraphPortMismatch.ts) returns `mismatch: false` when a **crossing** port has zero referring exterior edges, which tolerates exactly the state (a) and (b) say does not exist. **THE TRAP, and it would be an easy and damaging misread of (a) and (b).** **`exteriorRelationLabel?: string` on `EphemeraCrossingPort` STAYS OPTIONAL.** Its optionality is about `kind === 'Custom'` carrying a label and other kinds having none --- the guard requires it non-empty for `Custom` and permits absence otherwise --- **and has nothing to do with whether a port has an exterior endpoint. Do not make this field required.** **CONSUMERS, surveyed in code 2026-09-15 and split by which sense of *presence* they use.** **Group A --- presence as a PORT kind; these change or disappear under [PN-8](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s *both*:** [`presenceSubGraph.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) (the cut and the carry-into-every-bucket rule), [`applyStepSequenceCore.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/applyStepSequenceCore.ts) (the character single-binding validator), [`containmentPopulationSteps.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/containment/containmentPopulationSteps.ts), [`kernelStep.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/kernelStep.ts) (a step type typed `EphemeraPresencePort`), [`presencePortStepsForMove.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/compile/presencePortStepsForMove.ts), [`mergeReducer.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/mergeReducer.ts)'s `isCrossingPort`, `classifyLudicGraphPortMismatch.ts`'s PR-15 branch, [`findRelationalChain.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/synthesize/findRelationalChain.ts) (collapses to `if (!port)`), and the `kind === 'Present'` branch of `isEphemeraLudicGraphPort` in [`ephemeraMeta.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts). **Group B --- presence as an EDGE kind; UNAFFECTED, since clause 6 keeps `Present` edges:** `compileRelationalFromSkeleton.ts`, `groundChange.ts`, and `presenceSubGraph.ts`'s `edge.kind` filters. **Group B is easy to over-delete while doing Group A; the two groups are the reason this survey is recorded rather than re-derived.** **One incidental finding, no work owed:** `findRelationalChain` deliberately lumps `!port` (dangling address) with `kind === 'Present'` (atomic fact) on a shared outcome with the reason stated in-comment --- the two cases the docs split in 2026-08-26. Not a defect; under *both* it collapses to `if (!port)` and the doc split simply stops having a code counterpart. **--- 2026-09-16: this row's VERDICT stands, but its Group A / Group B CONSUMER PARTITION is falsified by the course correction and is redrawn in [PN-14](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), not here.** Group B was carved out as *presence-as-an-edge-kind, survives untouched* **on the express premise that clause 6 keeps `Present` edges** --- clause 6 no longer does. **Group A is unaffected and remains exactly as surveyed here, paths and all.** **Do not repair the partition by inverting it** (*Group B is now in scope*): that conflates the `'Present'` **port** kind with the `'Present'` **edge** kind, two senses of one literal that both live in shipped code, and PN-14 surveys them separately for exactly this reason. | 0, 3 | **Decided 2026-09-15 --- amend P3, not H3 clause 1. Group A/B consumer partition falsified 2026-09-16; redrawn in [PN-14](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)** |
| **PN-10** | **Does [PQ-9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md)'s deferred serde boundary come due here?** PQ-9 holds that the parsed shape IS the stored shape --- no string form, no separator parsing, in the domain layer --- and defers the serde/Dynamo string boundary to a later slice, **explicitly gated on *a port address needing to stand free of an edge*.** ~~**A presence node standing free of any edge is that condition, exactly.**~~ **So the question is whether the deferral is expiring on its own stated trigger rather than being overridden** --- which is a different, cheaper conversation than reopening PQ-9. **Check the gate's wording before treating this as a new decision**, and settle it with [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), since the id spelling and the string boundary are the same problem seen from two sides. **--- amendment 2026-09-15: the gate does not fire, and this proposal moves it further away rather than closer.** The struck sentence substitutes *node* for *port address* in the gate's own wording. PQ-9's trigger is **a port address needing to stand free of an edge**, and under [PN-8](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s `both` a presence binding is no longer a port address in either structure --- so a free-standing presence node is not an instance of the gated condition, it is the removal of the only construct that was ever a candidate for it. **[PN-9](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) makes that explicit:** retiring P3's default 2 (*port with no exterior edge = pure presence indicator*) deletes the one sanctioned port-with-no-edge, leaving every surviving port a crossing port, which P3 defines as having exactly one interior edge and one exterior referrer --- co-minted with its edge, never free of one. **The domain layer's shape confirms this independently of any plan row:** [`ephemeraMeta.ts:249`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts) stores a port address as structured `{ owner, port }`, and its own comment at line 231 states the deferral verbatim; `grep` for a string-form port address (`CONTRAPTION#`) across every `.ts` in the repo returns **zero** hits. **And the boundary the gate protects does not exist yet:** `ludicCache` is four in-memory files with no Dynamo writer --- the record key (`LCACHE#<host>` or otherwise) is settled in the abstraction-layers plan at its Slice line 88 but unimplemented. **So the author's reading is right: PQ-9 comes due when we serialize `ludicCache` entries, and not before.** **One consequence to carry to [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), which is why the two rows were paired:** its third option (a synthetic tagged id, `OBJECT#CONTRAPTION#{id}`) is disfavoured for a reason stronger than the `isEphemeraTaggedId` throw PN-5 already names --- **it would reintroduce into the domain layer exactly the string form PQ-9 abolished there**, making the separator question live again in the one layer that had been cleared of it. The throw is the symptom; the layering violation is the argument. **Whichever of PN-5's first two options wins, nothing here parses a string, so this row closes without constraining that choice.** | 2 | **Answered 2026-09-15 --- gate does not fire; PQ-9 stays deferred to `ludicCache` serde** |
| **PN-11** | **Does [P8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#proposal-p8-the-edge-as-an-entity--independent-existence-identity-and-multi-graph-occurrence) need to land with this, before it, or not at all?** P8 --- *the edge as an entity: independent existence, identity and multi-graph occurrence* --- is the sibling proposal to reifying presence, and the author proposed a form in which edges are minted separately from any graph with `from`/`to` as their own fields, the graph's edge list becoming references. ~~**The coupling is real: an edge entity living outside any graph must name its endpoints in terms meaningful outside any graph, and `{owner, port}` is host-local.** **A presence node is the one terminal that is global by construction**, which is why the two proposals attract each other.~~ **--- amendment 2026-09-15: the globality claim is FALSE, and it is false on this proposal's own text.** [Clause 1](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses) addresses a presence node as `OBJECT#CONTRAPTION#{presence node id}` --- **that prefix does exactly the work `owner` did in `{owner, port}`, and an id that were globally meaningful would not need it.** [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) says the same from the other side: the node carries a minted uuid **and a reference to the host it is presence for**. **A presence node is intensely local to its graph; the migration changes the mechanism, not the scope of the name.** **The conflation to avoid, since it is an easy one:** a uuid is globally **unique** (it will not collide), which is not the same as globally **resolvable** (nameable without already knowing which graph to look in). **P8 needs the second; a uuid supplies only the first** --- if the sole path to a presence node is *load the host's graph and look in its node list*, the reference is host-local however unique the id. **Where the word probably came from:** in a merge, presence nodes from many hosts coexist in one node list, so their ids must not collide **across hosts** --- real, and needed, but that is cross-host uniqueness upgraded to globality. **Consequence for this row: the coupling premise falls, and with it the claimed attraction between the two proposals**, since reifying presence does not supply the global endpoint name P8 wants. **They are separable and should stay separable unless this row finds otherwise** --- P8 is structural and unresolved in its own file, and making this migration depend on it converts a bounded migration into an open-ended one; **with the attraction argument gone, separable is now the plain reading rather than the hoped-for one.** **Decide only: does anything in Slices 2--6 foreclose P8?** Not whether P8 is right. **One thing this amendment retires:** a worry raised in conversation 2026-09-15 while weighing [PN-8](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) --- that reifying in `ludicGraph` might trip **[PR-4](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s exclusion (4)** (a presence edge whose target's `owner` is not a node in the same graph, forbidden until a case asks) --- **rested on this same false globality and does not survive it.** Nested alignment becomes `room_presence_node -[Present]-> GHG#{presence node id}`, where `GHG` is still a node in the room's graph: same locality and same shape PR-4 already licenses. **Never written into this file; recorded here so it is not rediscovered as an open cost.** **CHECK PERFORMED 2026-09-15, against P8's own text rather than this row's gloss of it. Verdict: NOTHING in Slices 2--6 forecloses P8, and the row closes.** **Why it is safe to answer now, with the slices still moving:** foreclosure asks whether this migration **commits to a property P8 would have to undo**, which is graded against the invariants the migration adopts --- **not** against current slice contents, which would make the answer expire every time a `PN-` row landed. **P8 iteration 1 is `chainId?: string` on every leg plus [the comparison rule](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule), and it explicitly builds *"no Edge record, no reverse index, NO PRESENCE FOR EDGES, and no mint or strip operation."*** Slices 2--6 add a node kind, relocate a completeness flag, and work the `Present` cover; **they touch no relational-leg identity field, and `Present` edges carry no `edgeId` at all** (two deliberately unrelated identity schemes, [`AGENT.edges.md`](../../../../../packages/mtw-wml/ts/standardize/keys/edges/AGENT.edges.md#list-typing-note)). **P8's entity half needs two things and this migration obstructs neither:** an **extensible terminal union** (a chain may be an edge's terminal) --- [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) widens that union and thereby sets the precedent P8 would reuse, which is the opposite of foreclosing --- and **referents hostable apart from their legs**, on which this migration commits nothing, since [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) keeps edges out of the cover entirely. **No `P8-i1-dependent` tag is owed:** this is a non-dependency finding, and [the tagging test](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) warns against exactly that over-tag. **TWO CONTACT POINTS, recorded rather than waved past, because neither is foreclosure and both are places a later editor could create one.** **(1) [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) is touched from both sides** --- [PN-6](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) amends its **node-domain** clause, and P8 lists *PR-9's cover* among the four lanes its consequences land in, bearing on the **edge** clause. **Different clauses of one row; whoever moves it must check both.** **(2) The word *chain* is overloaded across two schemes** --- the cache's `chains` field ([PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), and the rebuild plan's D8 on its name) versus P8's chain-as-independent-referent. **PN-2's firewall --- *re-graining does not by itself make a cache edge chain-shaped, that is D9* --- is what keeps them apart and MUST SURVIVE any re-grain.** **RE-OPEN TRIGGER, stated:** this closure is void if a slice ever makes a presence node **a terminal of a chain**, or gives the cache's `chains` field **P8's identity semantics** rather than route bookkeeping. **Neither is in Slices 2--6 as scoped.** | 2 | **Answered 2026-09-15 --- no foreclosure** |
| **PN-12** | **Referential integrity, inherited from the rebuild plan's [3d](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#recommended-order).** [`isEphemeraLudicCacheData`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/types.ts) validates nodes and edges **independently** --- an edge naming an absent node type-checks --- and *nothing validates the marker's honesty*. **This proposal improves both and closes neither.** Clause 3's *zero or all* is checkable; an edge to an absent presence node is checkable; **but `consolidated: true` on a materialized presence node can still lie about its members**, which is the marker-honesty half unchanged. **And [Slice 6](#recommended-order) makes an edge-to-an-absent-node *legal by design*, which is a direct conflict with the naive form of 3d's check** --- the check must distinguish *absent presence node* (a signal) from *absent component node* (corruption). **Decide the check's shape here rather than inheriting 3d's framing**, which was written before absence carried meaning. **[PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s cross-shard half forwards here 2026-09-15:** whether an intervening host can be unmaterialized between a node and the bucket it is attributed to is the same question as *absent presence node (signal) versus absent component node (corruption)*, and is not a separate row. **--- WIDENED 2026-09-16 by the course correction, and the row stays Open with a SECOND reference shape to answer, not a replacement one.** The shape above (an edge reaching an unmaterialized presence node) **survives unchanged** --- [PR-15](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) keeps non-`Present` edges landing on presence bindings, so it is untouched by the removal of `Present` edges. **The new one runs the other way: a `cover` entry naming a component node that is not in `nodes`.** *Absent presence node* still reads as **signal** (*a binding exists, was not pulled*); *a covered member absent from a pulled bucket* reads as **corruption** --- **but only under [PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)(i).** Under PN-17(ii)'s union rule the very same state is an ordinary **partial** cover and must be tolerated. **So this row cannot be answered before PN-17, and the two must not give opposite readings of one stored state.** **[PN-16](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) bears on it too** --- its sub-question (b), whether a partial answer must be representable on the MEMBER side, is this question asked from the other index, **and if PN-16 and PN-12 diverge, one of the two registers will be enforcing an invariant the other permits breaking.** **--- 2026-09-16: [PN-16](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is DECIDED (i), and this row INHERITS its answer rather than re-deciding it.** The author's construction ruling --- **the presence cover is the unit of granularity by which nodes are materialized** --- makes an uncovered cached node **unreachable**, so **the new shape resolves: a `cover` entry naming a node absent from `nodes` is CORRUPTION, never a tolerated partial.** **There is no second register left to diverge from**, which discharges the divergence warning above by removing one of its two halves. **What STAYS open here is the FIRST shape only** --- an edge reaching an unmaterialized presence node --- **which is [Slice 6](#recommended-order)'s *binding exists, was not pulled* signal and is still a signal rather than a break.** **And note the two shapes now have opposite verdicts, which is the thing to get right when building the check:** absent **presence node** = signal; absent **covered member** = corruption. **Still gated on [PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s first half**, which decides whether a cache `cover` is complete-or-absent --- **and PN-16's construction is strong evidence for complete-or-absent, since a partial cover would reintroduce exactly the unreachable state just ruled out.** **--- refined the same day: PN-17 gained a third candidate, (iii) replace-and-adjust, and it does NOT weaken that evidence** --- (iii) keeps every cover **total**, so *a `cover` entry naming an absent node is corruption* holds under (i) and (iii) alike and fails only under (ii). **What (iii) DOES change here is the lifecycle, not the verdict:** under it a node may be **dropped** by a superseding cover, so this row's check must run **after** the cull rather than against a mid-update state --- otherwise a node correctly being removed reads as corruption for the duration of the fold. **That is the same *never observe a mid-drop state* discipline D11's single-chokepoint rule already establishes**, and it is the reason (iii) does not multiply this row's work. **--- 2026-09-16: PN-17 DECIDED (iii), so this row is no longer gated on it.** **What it inherits is settled: a `cover` entry naming a node absent from `nodes` is CORRUPTION** (covers stay total under (iii)), **while an edge reaching an unmaterialized presence node stays a SIGNAL.** **And the lifecycle caveat above is scoped away for now rather than owed:** PN-17 defers the update-cull to the warm-update iteration, so **under iteration 1 nothing is ever dropped and there is no mid-update state for this check to run against.** **Build the check straightforwardly; revisit the after-the-cull ordering when PN-17's re-open trigger fires** (*the first path that writes a `ludicCache` without full reconstitution*). **WHAT REMAINS OPEN HERE is the referential-integrity work itself** --- rebuild 3d, inherited not duplicated --- **not a question about which reading applies.** **--- DECIDED 2026-09-16 BY THE AUTHOR, and this row CLOSES. Its marker-honesty half decomposes into THREE questions of which only one was ever this row's, and the check's shape is settled.** **MARKER HONESTY IS THREE QUESTIONS, NOT ONE --- the original framing (*`consolidated: true` can still lie about its members*) ran them together, which is exactly why it read as an unclosable hole.** **(1) INTERNAL CONSISTENCY --- does the cache contradict ITSELF?** A `cover` entry naming a node absent from `nodes`. **Checkable, and settled above as CORRUPTION. This is the only one of the three that belongs to a referential-integrity check at all.** **(2) EXTERNAL FIDELITY --- does `consolidated: true` still match the `ludicGraph`'s bucket NOW?** **This is CACHE COHERENCE, and no cache can answer it from inside:** information was copied at a moment, and an invalidation may be in flight toward you. **It is addressed by invalidation and rebuild, never by a validator --- asking the guard to catch it is asking it to see something it structurally cannot.** **MOOT IN ITERATION 1**, which reconstitutes wholesale ([PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)): a cache that is always rebuilt is never stale. **It goes live at PN-17's EXISTING re-open trigger --- *the first path that writes a `ludicCache` without full reconstitution* --- and gets NO new row, being the same trigger on the same event.** **(3) WRITE-TIME COMPLETENESS --- did the producer write a COMPLETE cover at the moment it set `consolidated: true`?** A short-but-internally-consistent cover is invisible to (1) and is not staleness either. **This is a PRODUCER CONTRACT, it needs no new mechanism, and it is ALREADY ASSIGNED:** [Slice 7](#recommended-order) states it --- *"The single compile path is what makes D3's completeness claim reliable --- preserve that, or the binding enumeration stops being trustworthy and clause 3 has nothing to stand on."* **The house precedent is [D11](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s `[]` verdict and this follows it deliberately** --- *not compiler-checkable ... the guarantee rests entirely on that chokepoint being the only writer* --- **accepted with a NAMED FALSIFIER rather than papered over by a check that cannot see what it would be checking.** **FALSIFIER, in D11/TP-2's style and naming a PROPERTY rather than a population: falsified if a `consolidated: true` presence node can be written by any path that did not enumerate that bucket in the SAME operation.** **A second producer honouring the pairing falsifies nothing; a single producer split into two phases breaks it on its own.** **THE CHECK'S SHAPE, which is what this row reserved for itself: TIGHTEN [`isEphemeraLudicCacheData`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/types.ts) --- do NOT add a separate validator.** **The defect is in that guard's own structure** --- it validates `nodes` and `edges` **independently**, so an edge naming an absent node type-checks --- **and a second validator beside it would leave the hole in the guard callers actually reach for.** **TWO ASSERTIONS WITH OPPOSITE VERDICTS, which is the thing to get right when building it:** a `cover` entry naming a node absent from `nodes` **FAILS**; an edge terminating at an **absent presence node PASSES**, that being [Slice 6](#recommended-order)'s *binding exists, was not pulled* signal and **legal by design.** **BUILD IT IN [Slice 6](#recommended-order) WITH 3d, NOT BEFORE --- `cover` is not a field until [Slice 4](#recommended-order)**, so until then there is nothing to validate. **--- AMENDMENT 2026-09-17: one word in the internal-consistency check, and it is load-bearing.** The check reads *a host **ONE OR MORE** of whose covers names this presence node* --- **never *exactly one***. [PN-22](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) asks whether one presence node may be covered by more than one parent binding (tree versus DAG) and is **OPEN**; **writing the quantifier as *exactly one* would settle it silently, by making the DAG fail an integrity check instead of by costing it.** Under the tree reading *one or more* is simply never exercised past one, so this costs that branch nothing. **A second and weaker direction is worth keeping alongside it: `fromHostId` must name a host one of whose covers names this node** --- a coarse claim checkable against a fine one, which is what survives of the conflict rule now that [PN-21](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) deliberately holds the two fields at different grains. **--- AMENDED 2026-09-17+ by [PN-23](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), and it is a POINTER correction, not a decomposition change.** The write-time clause is discharged by **the single compile path**, and [PN-23](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) moved that path's last edit from [Slice 7](#recommended-order) to [Slice 7a](#recommended-order) --- so the clause discharges at **7a**. **The three-way decomposition is otherwise untouched:** internal settled, external still CACHE COHERENCE riding [PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s trigger, and this row still BUILDS in [Slice 6](#recommended-order). **Resist the reading that the write-time clause belongs with the FOLD** (Slice 5's `cover` expansion) --- it was weighed and it is wrong for this row: the clause is about *one writer*, and the fold is a cache-side producer that the single-compile-path argument never ranged over. | 4, 6 | **Decided 2026-09-16 --- tighten `isEphemeraLudicCacheData`, not a separate validator: cover-names-absent-node FAILS, edge-to-absent-presence-node PASSES. Marker honesty decomposed: internal settled, external is CACHE COHERENCE riding PN-17's trigger, write-time discharged by ~~Slice 7~~ **[Slice 7a](#recommended-order)**'s single compile path (pointer amended 2026-09-17+, PN-23) with a named falsifier. Builds in Slice 6** |
| **PN-13** | **In `ludicGraph`, an unstated membership relation *is* `In`.** **DECIDED 2026-09-15 BY THE AUTHOR**, and recorded as its own item because it is a claim about `ludicGraph` semantics that outlives this migration. If a node is a member of a host and has not been designated `PartOf` (or `On`), then it is `In` that host: the explicit edge and its absence carry **the same semantic content**, and the edge is an optional statement of *how*, not the record *that*. **What makes this cheap rather than a reinterpretation of live data: `In` is never minted in production today.** The deterministic parser treats containment language (`in`/`inside`/`into`) as **recognized-but-unsupported** and always defers ([`relationalTemplates.ts:68`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/deterministicTemplate/relationalTemplates.ts), with `normalizeRelationSpan.ts` doing the same post-Parse); the one production producer of a rehost payload hardcodes [`containment: 'On'`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/actions/enrich/objectManipulation/compileObjectRehostFromSkeleton.ts); and `PartOf` is minted only by `containmentPopulationSteps` for authored Room-in-Area / Feature-in-Room nesting. **So the live distribution is: `PartOf` = authored structure, `On` = the only mobile hosting edge actually written, `In` = typed and plumbed end to end but never written, unstated = every drop and every take-hold.** This reading **explains the vestigial `In`** rather than leaving it an oddity, which is the main evidence for it. **It sits correctly inside [AB-53](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#settled-register):** the implied edge runs member -> root and is root-incident, so the star topology is exactly what it would materialize to. **Scope, settled with it: the `Character` host is included, and `In` there means *in their possession / inventory*, the ordinary MUD sense** --- not that the object has been swallowed. **Object hosts need no ruling**, since the only route putting an object inside an object is `rehost`, which always states a kind. **RE-OPEN TRIGGER, stated because one enum value is carrying two meanings:** if possession ever wants its own hosting kind (`Held`/`Carried`), the Character case moves out from under this reading and this row is re-graded --- it is not re-argued for Rooms. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT SETTLE, and the distinction is load-bearing: in `ludicGraph` the equivalence is free *precisely because it is inert*.** Nothing in `ludicGraph` derives membership from edges --- membership is `nodeIds`, and the adjacency index agrees (`getMembershipContainers` indexes **node placement**, not edge references) --- so declaring the reading changes no code, no data and no invariant. **That inertness does not travel.** ~~In `ludicCache` the hosting edge is the *carrier*, so an implicit `In` only does work there if the merge materializes it, **which [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) considered and rejected in favour of writing `Present` at mint time.**~~ **--- superseded by clause 2 below, and the struck sentence named only two options where there are three.** It is true that [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) considered and rejected **materializing** hosting edges. What it missed is that PN-1's own remedy supplies a third route: once every consolidated bucket states its members with explicit `Present` links, **the `Present` cover is the carrier**, and no hosting edge needs materializing for the reading to do work. **Kept struck as a caution about sequencing rather than deleted: the sentence was correct when written and stopped being correct the moment PN-1 landed in the same conversation.** ~~**This row is therefore `ludicGraph`-only by decision, not by oversight**~~, and its obligation is documentary: [Slice 0](#recommended-order) writes it into [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md)'s containment section, beside AB-53's star paragraph it qualifies. **--- SECOND CLAUSE ADDED 2026-09-15, same conversation: the reading DOES extend to `ludicCache`, in a form that was unavailable until [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) landed.** **Clause 2: in `ludicCache`, a node with a `Present` link from the presence node associated with root R, and no `On`/`PartOf` edge to R, is `In` R.** **Why this is sound where clause 5 was not, and it is the whole argument: the arrow is reversed.** Clause 5 derived the **total** record (bucket membership) from the **partial** one (hosting edges), which fails when the partial record is mostly empty. This derives the **partial** record (the *how*) from the **total** one (the `Present` cover, total by [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)). **Partial-from-total is fine; total-from-partial was the defect.** **It also stays a READING, never a write, which is what keeps [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s two objections from reaching it** --- PN-1 rejected *materializing* `In` edges (fabricated source-vocabulary data in the cache; the one-way door collapsing *author said `In`* into *merge inferred `In`*), and neither objection applies to a rule a consumer applies at read time. **The `Present` link simply plays the role that node-list membership plays in clause 1.** **FOUR CONDITIONS, each already owned by a row on this board.** **(1) Exclude the root.** PR-9 puts the root in every bucket, exempt from needing an edge to establish it; without the exclusion the rule yields `R is In R`. **(2) Exclude presence nodes --- GATED ON [PN-6](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), WHICH IS STILL OPEN.** If a presence node is a member of the cover it fronts, it acquires an implicit `In`, which is wrong *in kind*: a presence node is not *in* anything, it **is** the index. **(3) Scoped to consolidated buckets, and silence is not a negative.** A node whose bucket was not pulled has no `Present` link, so the rule yields **nothing** --- correct, but a consumer must never read *no implicit `In`* as *not `In`*. That is [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s absence-as-signal versus absence-as-corruption arriving in a second place. **(4) It reads the presence node's host-reference field** (clause 1's *what am I presence for*). The field exists in every one of [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s candidate shapes so this does not block it, **but the field now has a second reader, which is mild input to PN-5's arm question.** **ONE ECHO, NAMED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE REDISCOVERED AS AN OBJECTION.** The rule infers from an **absence**, which is structurally what [P3's retired default 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) did (*"the absence of an edge **is** the signal"*) and rhymes with [PR-15](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s self-heal bug, where `edgesReferringToPort` inferred a **declared** property from a **pattern**. **The difference is real but thin, so it is stated here rather than left for whoever hits the PR-15 precedent and gets nervous: the absence is not *evidence* of `In`, it **is** `In`, by this row's clause 1. A declaration, not an inference.** **WHAT IT BUYS, recorded because it is the reason to bother:** the cache becomes uniformly answerable for *how is M related to R* with no no-edge special case at the consumer --- which, with `ludicCache` headed for prompt construction, is the difference between narration having a relation to render for every member and having to handle a silent fourth state. | 0 | **Decided 2026-09-15 --- unstated is `In`; clause 1 `ludicGraph`, clause 2 `ludicCache` as a read-time rule (never materialized), gated on [PN-6](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) for the presence-node exclusion** |
| **PN-14** | **Does `'Present'` retire from `HostRelationalEdgeKind`, and at which slice?** **The literal has TWO distinct senses in shipped code and the course correction kills both, which is why this cannot be answered by grepping the name.** As an **edge** kind it is the cover mechanism --- [`presenceSubGraph.ts:64`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) filters `edge.kind === 'Present'` to read membership, and line 240's `contentEdges` filters it back out. As a **port** kind it is the presence binding itself --- `EphemeraPresencePort.kind` at [`ephemeraMeta.ts:540`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts), minted by
