| **PN-15** | **Are `cover` and `consolidated` two fields or one?** Clause 1 now has the presence node carrying both: `consolidated: boolean` (*this bucket's interior was pulled*) and `cover` (*these are its members*). **One field can carry both facts** --- `cover` absent means *not consolidated*, `cover: []` means *consolidated and empty* --- and that is exactly the state [the banked-losses paragraph](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses) already reasoned about when it defeated **CC0's box-can-be-empty argument** with *the marker is the node, not its contents*. **For ONE field:** the two-field form admits a **fourth state that means nothing** (`consolidated: false` alongside a non-empty `cover`), and an invariant nothing checks is the failure mode [Slice 4](#recommended-order) already calls out by name. **For TWO fields:** absence-as-meaning is the *no value* versus *not applicable* conflation [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) spent a whole verdict removing from `chains`, and which [Slice 2](#recommended-order) then refused to reintroduce one field over (the `shortName: ''` rejection) --- **an optional field whose undefined-ness is load-bearing is that same hazard wearing the opposite sign, and this plan has now paid for it twice.** **Weigh those two against each other directly; do not settle it on field count or bytes.** **Sequencing note that can decide the row outright: the collapse is only AVAILABLE if [PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) makes a consolidated cover complete.** Under a union/partial merge rule, *absent* and *not yet filled* stop being distinguishable, the one-field form loses its discriminating power, and this row answers itself. **So read PN-17 first.** **--- WIDENED 2026-09-16 by [clause 7](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses): this row is now about THREE facts, not two, and the third may absorb the question.** `fullCoverage` joins `consolidated` and `cover` on one record, and **three near-named per-binding fields is the state [Slice 4](#recommended-order) already warns produces fields read as each other.** **[PN-19](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s sentinel-arm option is the one that interacts hardest here:** if full coverage is an **arm of `cover`** rather than a sibling boolean, then `cover` becomes a small discriminated union and **the disagreement states this row exists to prevent become unrepresentable for the clause-7 case too**, not merely forbidden. **Decide PN-19 and PN-15 together, or the record grows a field per question asked.** **--- DECIDED 2026-09-16 BY THE AUTHOR, together with [PN-19](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) as this row required. Two parts.** **(1) PN-19(b) DISSOLVES the three-field problem outright.** With full coverage as an **arm of `cover`**, full-coverage and an enumerated cover are **ALTERNATES, never co-present** --- there is no pair to hold in agreement, so the *fourth state that means nothing* objection never arises between them, and **this row reduces to the question it originally asked: is `consolidated` explicit, or encoded as the absence of `cover`?** **(2) KEEP `consolidated` AS EXPLICIT BOOKKEEPING.** Absence-as-meaning is **not** made to carry it. **And the state this row called meaningless is GIVEN A MEANING rather than forbidden --- which is what makes the two-field form safe rather than merely tolerable:** `consolidated: false` with a **non-empty `cover`** reads as ***preview* information --- what the cover is in the `ludicGraph`, with NO bearing on authorizing anything in this `ludicCache`.** **THE STANDING CONSEQUENCE, and it is a build rule across [Slices 4](#recommended-order)--[6](#recommended-order): every check that consults cover information FOR AUTHORIZATION must filter on `consolidated: true`.** **That is good discipline independently of this verdict** --- it makes the authorizing predicate say what it means at each call site, instead of resting on a preview happening not to be there --- **and it is precisely the kind of rule missed at one site out of six, so write it into the doc comment where the filter lives rather than leaving it here.** **WHAT PREVIEW CANNOT BE, falling out of PN-19(b) and recorded so its absence is not later read as a defect:** the cache's `cover` takes **the list arm only**, so a preview is always an **enumerated list** --- **a graph-side FULL cover therefore has no preview form in a cache**, expansion being exactly what the fold does when the bucket is pulled. | 4 | **Decided 2026-09-16 --- keep `consolidated` explicit; PN-19(b) makes full-coverage and `cover` alternates, so no third field arises. `consolidated: false` + non-empty `cover` = graph-side PREVIEW, authorizing nothing; authorization checks filter on `consolidated: true`** |
| **PN-16** | **Is `cover` the same fact as the rebuild plan's [D11](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) node-side endpoint register?** D11 decided **two registers** --- `supportedBy` on the edge (traversal justification) and a node-side register answering *which binding put this node in this cache* --- and **deliberately left the second's element shape open**. `cover` answers *which component nodes does this binding cover*. **These are one relation and its transpose**, so at most one needs storing and the other is an index over it. **Three candidate answers: (i) `cover` subsumes the register** --- the endpoint fact is recoverable by scanning presence nodes, and D11's register is never built; **(ii) the register subsumes `cover`** --- membership lives on the member, and the presence node keeps only `consolidated`; **(iii) both**, as a deliberate denormalization with a stated invariant that they agree. **What should decide this is not elegance but three concrete things.** **(a) Which direction the cut actually queries:** [`nodesFromPresencePort`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) asks *give me this binding's members*, which is `cover`'s direction and is the hot path. **(b) Whether a PARTIAL answer must be representable on the member side** --- a component node materialized into a cache whose presence node was not --- which (ii) can express and (i) cannot; **this is the same question [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) asks and the two must not answer it differently.** **(c) Merge cost:** (iii) obliges `mergeReducer` to reconcile two structures that can disagree, which is precisely the *two mechanisms that agree by luck* failure [Slice 5](#recommended-order) names --- **so (iii) is not the safe compromise it looks like, and should be chosen only with the reconciliation rule written down.** **D11 IS A ROW IN ANOTHER, IN-FLIGHT PLAN: decide here, amend there by POINTER ([Slice 0b](#recommended-order)), and do not close D11 from this file** --- closing a sibling plan's row from here is the out-of-scope move [this plan already names](#what-this-plan-disturbs-and-what-it-leaves-alone). **Settle this before ANY [Slice 4](#recommended-order) code.** **--- NEW ARGUMENT 2026-09-16, created by [PN-18](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) and recorded here because it did not exist when this row was written: legalising MULTIPLE FULL COVERS puts a concrete asymmetry into (a).** If two bindings each cover the whole host, then **every** component node is covered by **both** --- so the register direction stores *N nodes x k bindings* while the `cover` direction stores *k flags*. **That is a compactness case for (i) `cover` subsumes the register**, and it grows with host size rather than being a constant-factor preference. **Do not let it decide the row alone**, because it argues only about the **`ludicGraph`** form: [clause 7](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses) makes full coverage illegal in `ludicCache`, where it expands, **so the cache sees the N x k form under either answer** and the compactness win is upstream of the structure this row is actually about. **Weigh it against (b)** --- whether a partial answer must be representable on the member side --- **which is still the sub-question that does the real work here.** **--- REFRAMED 2026-09-16 with the author, after reading [D11](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s own text rather than this row's summary of it. The row was asking a WEAKER question than the facts support.** **(1) `supportedBy` IS NOT IN THIS ROW AND MUST NOT BE SWEPT INTO IT.** D11 separates **traversal** (`supportedBy`, on the edge) from **endpoint** (on the node), and that separation rests on a **safety** argument rather than tidiness: [PN-2](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) kept [PN-11](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s re-open trigger from firing by arguing `supportedBy` is *"route bookkeeping in the `from`/`to`-**independent** `chains` list"*, while endpoint justification is `from`/`to`-**DEPENDENT** by definition --- so merging them *"would falsify the exact property that verdict rests on, silently re-opening a row recorded as closed."* **Nothing in the course correction touches any of that. `supportedBy` stays separate under every candidate below**, and a reader who arrives here via the word *register* should stop at this sentence. **(2) THE REAL QUESTION IS SHARPER THAN *are these transposes*: it is D11's OWN PREMISE.** D11's argument for **storing** the reverse index is quoted here because it is the entire hinge --- answering *given terminal Y, which binding put Y here* from D3's host-view shape *"means searching every host's binding-set for the one whose bucket contains Y --- and bucket membership is [PN-13](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s **read-time rule that is deliberately never materialized**. **The bridge is absent by decision, so the reverse index has to be stored.**"* **`cover` IS THAT BRIDGE, MATERIALIZED.** The correction turns bucket membership from a never-materialized read-time rule into a **stored field** --- **so D11's stated justification for the endpoint register is FALSIFIED, not outweighed**, and candidate (i) is no longer the thinly-argued option this row first wrote it as. **(3) What SURVIVES of D11, so this is not over-read as clearing the register away.** Its **cost** argument survives partially (*the node side rides an edit that is happening anyway*), since [PN-7](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) **relocates** D3's re-grain to clause 4's `consolidated` on the presence node rather than cancelling it. Its **serving** argument survives untouched: *a node-side register serves every edge touching that node instead of restating the fact once per edge.* **And (b) still decides the row** --- whether a **partial** answer must be representable on the member side, which is [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s question asked from the other index. **(4) D11 must be TOLD, not merely pointed at.** [Slice 0b](#recommended-order)'s item is upgraded accordingly: the message to that row is not *here is a candidate you were not weighed against* but ***your stated premise no longer holds***. **Still do not close D11 from this file.** **--- DECIDED 2026-09-16 BY THE AUTHOR: (i). `cover` SUBSUMES the endpoint register, and D11's node-side register is NOT built.** **The deciding move answers sub-question (b) by CONSTRUCTION rather than by weighing, and it is the author's: the presence cover is the UNIT OF GRANULARITY BY WHICH NODES ARE MATERIALIZED into a cache.** No path caches a node without at least one cover justifying it, and removing a cover removes every node for which it was the last justification. **--- SHARPENED 2026-09-16 by [PN-15](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), and the qualifier is load-bearing rather than pedantic: read *cover* here as a `consolidated: true` cover, throughout this verdict.** PN-15 gives `consolidated: false` with a non-empty `cover` the meaning ***preview of the graph-side cover, authorizing nothing in this cache*** --- **so a preview cover justifies NO node, and an unqualified reading of the construction would let one appear to.** **The construction is unchanged in force; it is now exact about which covers carry it.** **So the state (b) worried about --- a component node in the cache whose presence node is absent --- is UNREACHABLE, and `cover`'s inability to express it costs nothing.** **Traced rather than asserted:** a node Y is in the cache only if Y was in some pulled bucket, and pulling that bucket materializes its presence node carrying its cover (clause 6 writes it at mint time; [PN-6](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) clause (b) carries it into every cut). **TWO APPARENT EXCEPTIONS WERE RAISED AND BOTH DISSOLVED --- recorded because each looks like a counterexample and is not.** **(1) The root.** The **cache's own root needs no justification** --- it is the cache's identity, not a covered member. **Every other root is just a node, and it IS covered:** cutting host H's bucket for binding P puts H's own node in that bucket ([`nodesFromPresencePort`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) seeds the root), so H arrives in the parent's cache as part of P's cover. ***Root* is a role relative to a graph, not a property that survives into the merge --- the structure is SCALE-INVARIANT**, and a host node with cached contents is still just a node. **(2) [Slice 6](#recommended-order)'s referenced-only host node.** It is **justified by a cover on a PARENT, never by one inside itself** --- the error was looking inside H for a cover of H's own unpulled binding. **The mechanism already enforces it:** an edge from a node in bucket B to an H **not** in B is **stubbed** under [PN-6](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) clause (c)'s qualified/disqualified rule rather than carried whole, **so for H to be materialized at all it was already a covered member of some pulled bucket.** **WHAT WAS LEFT STANDING FOR THE REGISTER, and it was not enough.** D11's **stated premise** is falsified (above); its **cost** objection is defeated by its own scan reasoning (*two array passes over a single in-memory object ... no `ludicGraph` fetch, no second cache, no Dynamo round trip*); its ***rides an edit happening anyway*** argument **lapses entirely, since under (i) there is no register to build**; and its **serving** argument --- one node-side record serves every edge touching that node --- is a **convenience** claim, not a sufficiency one. **CONSEQUENCES. (a)** [Slice 4](#recommended-order)'s node-side endpoint register item is **RETIRED, not deferred**. **(b) [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) inherits this answer and must not diverge from it**, per this row's own standing instruction: under the construction, a `cover` entry naming a node absent from `nodes` is **corruption, never a tolerated partial**. **(c) `supportedBy` is untouched**, as it was under every candidate. **NOT DECIDED HERE, and still D11's own:** that row's other verdicts --- `[]` meaning *no traversal justification needed*, the single-chokepoint drop, the tagged-union rejection on typical-case record size, and TP-2's falsifier --- **all stand unaffected**, and [Slice 0b](#recommended-order) **reports to D11 rather than closing it.** | 4 | **Decided 2026-09-16 --- (i): `cover` subsumes the endpoint register, which is not built. (b) answered by CONSTRUCTION: the cover is the unit of materialization, so an uncovered cached node is unreachable** |
| **PN-17** | **What does a merge do with two `cover` fields for one presence node?** **Under edges this needed no rule** --- `mergeReducer` concatenated edges on an identity-key match and the structure carried the answer. **A field has no such default, and this is the one place the correction ADDS a question rather than removing one.** **The fork: (i) complete-or-absent** --- a consolidated bucket's `cover` is total over its members, two entries for one presence node must **agree**, and disagreement is an integrity break to throw on; **(ii) union** --- `cover` may be partial and the merge accumulates. **(i) is what clause 3's completeness claim wants** (*any presence node means the binding set is complete*) and what [D3](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s bookkeeping rests on. **(ii) is what an incremental or warm fold may need** --- and [Slice 5](#recommended-order) carries a standing obligation **not to foreclose** the deferred warm-update initiative, whose accumulator is *an ordinary graph whose open ports are what remains unresolved*. **Check (i) against that obligation explicitly before choosing it**, because *do not foreclose* is exactly the sort of constraint a totality rule violates silently. **SECOND HALF, and it must land in the same verdict:** `nodesFromPresencePort`'s **`presencePortCount <= 1` short-circuit** returns `[...graph.nodeIds, root]`, which with a stored `cover` becomes **a second source for one answer**. Decide whether it is redundant-and-harmless (keep as an optimization, pinned by a test that it agrees with `cover`) or must be rewritten to read the field. **Do not split these two questions across verdicts:** a merge rule and a read short-circuit that disagree is the same defect [PN-6](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) already fixed once, when its two arms disagreed about membership. **Bears on [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) (whether a dangling `cover` entry is a break or a tolerated partial) and DECIDES the availability of [PN-15](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s one-field form.** **--- AMENDED 2026-09-16: [clause 7](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses) supplies a THIRD answer to the short-circuit half, and it is better than both of the two above.** Under `fullCoverage` the short-circuit's **condition** becomes the stored fact --- `presencePortCount <= 1` is arity, `fullCoverage` is the same conclusion **asserted** --- so there is **no second source to disagree with at all**, rather than a second source that is tested (option one) or rewritten (option two). **Do not adopt it without [PN-18](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only):** if `fullCoverage` is arity-restricted, the flag and the count are **the same test spelled twice**, and this collapses back to option one wearing a new name. **Clause 7 bears on the FIRST half too.** Expansion at the fold ([Slice 5](#recommended-order)) produces a cover that is **total over the host by construction**, which is direct evidence for **(i) complete-or-absent** and sits awkwardly under **(ii) union** --- *every node of the host, minus whatever has not arrived yet* is not a state (ii) can tell from a genuine partial. **Weigh it as evidence, not as a decision: the warm-update non-foreclosure obligation is unchanged and still has to be checked against (i).** **--- SECOND HALF CLOSED 2026-09-16 by [PN-18](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), and closed by DELETION rather than by either option this row offered.** With `fullCoverage` written at every arity, **the `presencePortCount <= 1` short-circuit is retired outright** --- so it is neither *redundant-but-tested* (option one) nor *rewritten to read the field* (option two), and **the third candidate recorded above is superseded too**: there is no surviving second source to hold in agreement, which is a stronger result than making two sources agree. **This row's *do not split the two questions across verdicts* instruction is DISCHARGED, not violated** --- the second question is answered, and what remains is a single question, so there is nothing left to split. **THIS ROW STAYS OPEN ON ITS FIRST HALF ONLY: (i) complete-or-absent versus (ii) union**, for two entries of one presence node in one cache. **Do not read the Status cell's "Open" as covering the short-circuit** --- that is exactly the mistake this file warns about when a row's body preserves superseded argument by design. **--- THIRD CANDIDATE ADDED 2026-09-16, the author's: (iii) SECOND COVER WINS, ADJUST NODES ACCORDINGLY --- and it is a REFINEMENT OF (i), not a pole between (i) and (ii).** **The distinction that keeps it out of (ii): every cover stays TOTAL.** (ii)'s real cost was permitting **partial** covers, which reintroduces exactly the state [PN-16](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) ruled unreachable; **(iii) permits nothing partial.** It changes only what happens when two **complete but different** answers meet --- (i) calls that corruption, (iii) says **the newer wins and node membership follows.** **Clause 3's completeness claim and [D3](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s bookkeeping survive untouched**, which is precisely what (ii) would have cost. **AND IT PLUGS A HOLE IN (i) THAT THIS ROW DID NOT NAME: (i) HAS NO STORY FOR UPDATE.** If a bucket's membership legitimately changes --- a node moves in or out --- re-folding yields a different cover, and **(i) reads a legitimate refresh as an integrity break.** **This is the outcome of the check this row already demanded:** it says to test (i) against [Slice 5](#recommended-order)'s **warm-update non-foreclosure obligation** before choosing it, and **the check comes back AGAINST (i)** --- a totality rule that cannot tell *refresh* from *corruption* is exactly how *do not foreclose* gets violated silently rather than by decision. **(iii) INVENTS NO MECHANISM**, which is the other half of its case: *adjust nodes accordingly* is [PN-16](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s construction applied to **update** rather than **removal** --- *removing a cover removes every node for which it was the last justification* --- and that is also [D11](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s single-chokepoint drop path. **It rides machinery the design already owes.** **TWO OBLIGATIONS IT CARRIES, neither optional.** **(1) *Second* must mean NEWER, not merely later-arriving.** Checked rather than assumed: `mergeReducer` is **accumulator-based** --- its own comments at [`mergeReducer.ts:297`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/mergeReducer.ts) and `:326` speak of *the accumulator* against *the bucket* --- **so incoming-versus-accumulated is structural for the fold, not argument order.** **But that guarantee belongs to the FOLD, not to the rule:** anywhere two independently-built caches meet, order is arbitrary and a **stale cover would silently regress membership.** **Either establish that accumulator-takes-newer always holds, or give the rule a sequence to compare --- and say WHICH, because a last-writer-wins rule with no defined *last* is the defect wearing the fix's clothes.** **(2) The cull must handle edges orphaned by dropped nodes.** Same machinery as D11's drop path, **but a SECOND invocation site** --- name it as such, or the drop path is written once for support removal and this path quietly has none. **--- DECIDED 2026-09-16 BY THE AUTHOR: (iii) REPLACE-AND-ADJUST, with its ordering obligation SCOPED to a later iteration.** **The rule: when two covers for one presence node meet, the NEWER WINS and node membership is adjusted to match.** Covers stay **total**, nothing partial is ever representable, **(i)'s totality survives and (ii) is rejected.** **THE SCOPING IS PART OF THE VERDICT, not a caveat bolted to it.** Obligation (1) above --- *assure the winning cover is the **newest** input* --- **is NOT owed by the first caching iteration, and IS owed by the later iteration that attempts cover-granular warm updates.** **Why that is a scoping rather than a promise, which is the part to check if this is ever re-argued: under full reconstitution the branch is UNREACHABLE.** Iteration 1 rebuilds the cache wholesale from one consistent snapshot of the `ludicGraph`s, so two covers for one presence node are **derived from the same source and cannot disagree** --- **(iii) and (i) are observationally identical there, and adopting (iii) costs iteration 1 nothing.** The newest-wins machinery **has no caller** until something writes a cache without reconstituting it. **WHAT ITERATION 1 DOES OWE IS *DO NOT FORECLOSE*** --- the obligation [Slice 5](#recommended-order) already carries for warm update --- **and writing the rule as *newer wins* rather than as *disagreement is corruption* IS the non-foreclosure.** A totality-violation throw would have to be **removed** later, and **removing a safety check is a worse edit than never writing one**, which is the whole reason (i) failed this row's own warm-update test. **RE-OPEN TRIGGER, checkable rather than aspirational: the first code path that writes a `ludicCache` WITHOUT full reconstitution.** None exists today --- [D11](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) records the same fact from the other side (*"no drop path exists in the code"*) --- **so the trigger is currently unfired and its condition is testable by inspection rather than taken on faith.** **ONE THING THAT MUST NOT DEFER BY ASSOCIATION, and it splits obligation (2) in half.** The cull *this rule* needs --- **edges orphaned by nodes a superseding cover dropped** --- **defers with (1)**, since under full reconstitution nothing is ever dropped. **But D11's OWN cull, triggered by SUPPORT REMOVAL rather than by update, is a different trigger on a different timetable and is NOT scoped by this verdict.** **Two invocation sites, two schedules --- do not read this deferral as covering both.** | 5, 6 | **Decided 2026-09-16 --- (iii) replace-and-adjust: the newer cover wins and nodes adjust. Ordering and update-cull obligations SCOPED to the warm-update iteration (unreachable under full reconstitution); iteration 1 owes only *do not foreclose*. D11's support-removal cull is NOT scoped by this. Second half closed the same day by PN-18: the short-circuit is deleted** |
| **PN-18** | **Is [clause 7](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses)'s `fullCoverage` restricted to arity <= 1?** **The cover is a COVER, not a partition** --- [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) settles totality over the indexed set, and **nothing anywhere forbids two bindings from covering the same node** --- so `fullCoverage: true` on one of three bindings is **representable**, meaning *this binding sees the whole host; the others see parts*. **The fork. (i) Restricted to arity <= 1:** clause 7 is then [PR-10](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) written down --- the same test, **stored rather than derived** --- and PR-10's *binding count, never host count* stays the discriminator. **(ii) Free at any arity:** clause 7 is then a **genuine widening** of PR-10, the arity test stops being the discriminator at all, and PR-10 owes a substantive amendment rather than a restatement. **[Slice 0b](#recommended-order)'s PR-10 item is blocked on this answer and cannot be written before it.** **The costs are not symmetric, and that is the argument to have.** Under **(i)** the flag is **derivable from arity and adds no information** --- which invites *why store it at all*, answered only by [PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only): storing it is what removes the second source. **So under (i) the whole value of clause 7 is PN-17's, and if PN-17 resolves another way clause 7 has no remaining justification** --- say that plainly rather than keeping a flag for its own sake. Under **(ii)** it adds real expressive power **and a new invariant nobody has stated yet: what a full-coverage binding coexisting with enumerated ones MEANS**, and whether the enumerated ones become redundant. **Answer that as part of this row** --- an encoding that permits a state no one can interpret is the defect this plan has spent three verdicts removing. **Test the fork against a real fixture rather than on generality, and [LC12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc12-the-spread-string) is already built for it:** the spread string, one end in the box, mid-span in the room, the other end in the bag. **Ask whether the room's binding is honestly *full coverage of the string* while the box and bag bindings cover parts** --- if that reading is sound, (ii) has a genuine use case and (i) would forbid a true statement; if it is not, (i) costs nothing. **Do not assume the answer from the single-binding motivating story.** **--- DECIDED 2026-09-16 BY THE AUTHOR, and the verdict is ONE statement covering both directions this row asked about separately: `fullCoverage` is ORTHOGONAL TO ARITY.** **(1) Arity does not RESTRICT it** --- option (ii). A host may carry full coverage at any arity, and **multiple full covers on one host are legal**, the cover being a cover and not a partition. **(2) Arity does not EXEMPT a binding from writing it** --- the author's extension of the question, and the half that does the work. A single-hosted node has full coverage **by construction**, and the flag is **written there too** rather than left implicit. **So the implicit form is RETIRED: clause 7 REPLACES [PR-10](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s implicit encoding rather than coexisting with it.** **The argument is not encoding size --- the explicit form is only slightly larger and much simpler --- and two concrete things drop out of it.** **(a) It removes the LAST arity branch in the design.** [PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) struck clause 5 on the stated principle *there is no arity-1 special case anywhere in the design*; `presencePortCount <= 1` is the only one that survived, on the correct ground that it states a **different** claim (*the bucket is the node list*) --- **but a fact the record now states outright does not need to be a branch.** **(b) It removes PR-10's call-site trap from every read path, and this is the real prize.** PR-10 records that *binding arity is **inherited** from the parent, so it is not readable off the thing itself*, and that *every call site must ask the binding list, never the host list, **even where hosting currently returns the right answer***. **With `fullCoverage` on the node, no read-path call site computes arity at all.** **Checked rather than assumed --- deleting the branch strands nothing:** both callers, [`presenceSubGraph.ts:90`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/presenceSubGraph.ts) and [`mergeReducer.ts:342`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/mergeReducer.ts), pass a **real `portId`**, so the guard's `<= 1` **zero arm is defensive and unreachable**, and PR-10's *the zero case behaves the same* is a claim about the concept, not about this branch. **Three consequences, forwarded rather than left to be rediscovered: [PN-17](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s second half CLOSES** (no short-circuit survives to be kept in agreement with anything); **[PR-10](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register) goes MOOT, NOT FALSE**, with its parked obligation forwarded ([Slice 0b](#recommended-order)); **and [Slice 3](#recommended-order)'s short-circuit item flips from *keep* to *delete*.** | 0b, 3 | **Decided 2026-09-16 --- orthogonal to arity in BOTH directions; the implicit form is retired** |
| **PN-19** | **How is full coverage encoded, and what makes it UNREPRESENTABLE in a `ludicCache` rather than merely forbidden there?** **One row, because the encoding largely decides the enforcement** --- split them and they end up inconsistent, which is this plan's most repeated failure shape. **Encoding candidates. (a) A sibling boolean**, `fullCoverage?: boolean` beside `cover`, as the author stated it. **(b) An arm OF `cover`** --- `cover: ComponentId[] \| { full: true }`, or a tagged equivalent --- so **the two facts cannot disagree**. **(c) `cover` absent plus `consolidated` true, meaning full** --- **reject this early; it is listed only so it is not rediscovered as clever.** It collides head-on with [PN-15](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), where absent-`cover` is the candidate spelling for *not consolidated*, and **one absence cannot carry both meanings.** **Enforcement follows from the choice, and this is the coupling.** Under **(b)** the cache's structure node simply takes the **list arm**: full coverage is **unrepresentable there by construction** --- no guard, no `Omit`, nothing anyone can forget, and [Slice 5](#recommended-order)'s expansion is **forced by the compiler at the fold**. Under **(a)** the two structure types must **diverge**, and the divergence is **not free today**: [`ludicCache/types.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicCache/types.ts) reuses `EphemeraLudicGraphStructureNode` as a **bare union member**, so as shipped the field would appear in the cache silently --- **exactly the silent-widening defect [PN-5](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) partitioned the node union to close, one type further along.** So (a) costs an `Omit` or a second declaration **plus** a runtime guard someone must remember to write, and *an invariant nothing checks* is the failure mode this family has hit before. **Weigh (a)'s readability honestly rather than treating (b) as obviously right:** a boolean named `fullCoverage` says what it means at a glance, a union arm does not, and **that is a real cost and the reason the author reached for (a) first.** **Whatever is chosen, the `isEphemeraLudicGraphStructureNode` / `isEphemeraLudicCacheNode` guards split in the SAME change** (the standing PQ-10 obligation). **Decide alongside [PN-15](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only), never before it** --- the two together fix how many fields this record has. **--- EXPANSION COST recorded 2026-09-16, after [PN-18](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) legalised multiple full covers, because it is the one real argument against [clause 7](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses)'s legality split and should be visible rather than discovered at build time.** Two full covers over a 500-node host expand to **1000 cache entries** --- **compactness is lost exactly where the graph had it**, and the loss scales with host size times binding count. **This does NOT reopen the illegal-in-`ludicCache` rule:** its ground is that *all nodes of the host* has **no referent** in a multi-host merge, which is [loss (A)](#why-this-is-opened) and is a correctness argument, not a size one --- **a cost does not answer a correctness claim.** **But it names a coupling with [PN-16](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) that neither row can see alone, and PN-16 should be read with it: *all nodes of host X* WOULD be well-defined in a merge if the node-side endpoint register existed to say which binding each node arrived through.** So **PN-16's (ii) or (iii) could in principle restore a compact cache form**, and **PN-16's (i) forecloses it.** **Do not treat that as a reason to choose (ii)** --- it is a consequence to price, and the compact cache form is not a stated requirement of anything today. **Recorded so the option is priced when PN-16 is decided, and not mourned afterward.** **--- DECIDED 2026-09-16 BY THE AUTHOR: (b), THE TYPED ARM. Full coverage is an ARM OF `cover`, not a sibling boolean.** **The author's reasoning INVERTS this row's own readability argument rather than merely outweighing it, which is why the argument should not be re-run later as though it were untested:** the codebase **already has enough places where the absence of a property encodes a semantic default to empty/false**, so an optional `fullCoverage?: boolean` is **not** the unambiguous thing (a) was credited with being --- it is one more instance of a pattern already carrying load elsewhere, **making (a) a LOOSE FIT for the house style rather than the plain-speaking option.** **With (a)'s only real advantage gone, (b)'s compile-time guarantees win uncontested.** **What follows structurally --- and it is exactly why this row bundled encoding with enforcement:** the cache's structure node takes **the LIST ARM ONLY**, so full coverage is **unrepresentable in a `ludicCache` by construction** --- **no `Omit`, no divergent declaration, no runtime guard for anyone to forget** --- and **[Slice 5](#recommended-order)'s expansion is FORCED BY THE COMPILER at the fold.** **Clause 7's *illegal in the cache* stops being a rule anyone must remember and becomes a thing that cannot be said.** **ONE BUILD NOTE, not a fork, and it must not be got wrong: TAG THE ARMS --- do NOT discriminate with `Array.isArray`.** A bare `ComponentId[] \| { full: true }` is separable only by **shape**, and ***branch on the declared kind, never on the pattern*** is [PR-15](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s own rule, earned by the `edgesReferringToPort` self-heal bug this plan cites twice already. **The `isEphemeraLudicGraphStructureNode` / `isEphemeraLudicCacheNode` guards split in the SAME change** (standing PQ-10 obligation). **(c) stays rejected, and (a) is not to be revived on readability grounds without first answering the house-style argument above.** | 3, 5 | **Decided 2026-09-16 --- (b) the typed arm: full coverage is a TAGGED arm of `cover`; the cache takes the list arm only, so illegality is structural and expansion is compiler-forced** |
| **PN-20** | **What is a `cover` entry --- a bare component id, or a `{ host, presence }` pair?** **DECIDED 2026-09-17 with the author: the PAIR.** **The deciding case is [PR-C1](AGENT.presence.corpus.planning.md#pr-c1-the-contraption-in-two-rooms-and-whether-covers-nest)'s Finding 4, and it FALSIFIES the bare form rather than merely disfavouring it.** `Contraption` holds two bindings (facing `ROOM#A` and `ROOM#B`); `GrapplingHookGun` is in both covers and holds two bindings of its own, **both carrying the same `fromHostId: OBJECT#Contraption`**. Under bare entries both parent covers name the same `GHG`, and both child bindings name the same parent host, so **which child binding pairs with which parent binding is stated NOWHERE** --- *"the nesting is representable; the alignment of nested covers is not."* **Global uniqueness of `universalKey` does not rescue it, and reaching for it is the wrong disambiguation:** the ambiguity is never about *which node*, it is about *which of that node's own covers*. **`fromHostId` provably cannot break the tie** --- the flashlight case's Finding 1 shows two bindings from one host are legal and that `fromHostId` cannot discriminate them. **This is PR-C1's OWN repair transposed to the new carrier:** that case settled *"give the presence edge a port-qualified terminal as its target… it names the child's port directly, **not merely the child's host**"*, and the 2026-09-16 course correction moved the carrier from the edge to `cover` **without moving the fact**. **The `host` component is NOT redundant with the presence id, and must not be optimized away in the cache.** In `ludicGraph` it is what keeps the referent **local** --- PR-C1's own answer to the cross-shard objection, *"`owner` is `GHG`, **already a node in Contraption's graph**"* --- where a bare `PRESENCE#{uuid}` would reach into the child's shard. In `ludicCache` it resolves either way, and **uniformity wins because [clause 7](#the-proposal-in-six-clauses) already forces one graph/cache divergence on this field; a second is how the two structures drift.** **Serde is NOT a gate, re-derived from [PQ-10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) rather than inherited from [PN-10](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s conclusion:** the hazard is a port address existing **as a string** where an id predicate can see it, and a structured value in a document body never becomes one. **FALSIFIER: a consumer that must name a covered node WITHOUT naming a binding**, which would make the qualification an obstacle rather than a refinement. **RE-OPEN TRIGGER, concrete and checkable today: anything forcing cover entries into strings** --- a DynamoDB String Set (String Sets hold only strings), or a record/index key built from an entry. Keep them structured in the document body and PQ-9's deferred boundary stays shut. **Costs [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s codomain a clarification ([Slice 0b](#recommended-order)), its domain having been restated only on 2026-09-16.** | 3, 4 | **Decided 2026-09-17 --- `{ host, presence }` pairs** |
| **PN-21** | **Does `fromHostId` upgrade to a binding-grained `fromPresence` alongside [PN-20](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)?** **DECIDED 2026-09-17 with the author: NO --- it stays exactly as shipped, and the RATIONALE is the durable half of this row.** **The symmetry argument was raised and defeated on what the two fields ASSERT.** `cover` asserts **the pairing**; `fromHostId` asserts **a LOCATION**. Different propositions --- so this is not one fact at two grains, and [D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s `chains` diagnosis (*"names a meaning the field has retired"*) does **not** transfer: `fromHostId`'s meaning is untouched by PN-20. **The cost model decides it.** A `ludicGraph` is sharded per host, so from a presence node inside a child the parent binding sits in another Dynamo row; `fromHostId` turns *scan everything* into **ONE READ**, after which finding the covering binding is an in-memory pass over that single host's presence nodes. **`fromPresence` would save that in-memory pass and NO additional read**, while adding a second statement of the pairing that can drift. In `ludicCache` it saves nothing at all and merely narrows a scan --- **an idiom [D11](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) already priced as cheap** (*"two array passes over a single in-memory object"*). So the fine grain earns nothing on either side of the graph/cache line. **A SECOND REASON, load-bearing rather than incidental: a single-valued `fromPresence` PRESUPPOSES THE TREE.** Under [PN-22](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s DAG reading it would have to become `fromPresence[]`, re-importing the very multiplicity it was meant to name. **Keeping `fromHostId` coarse is what leaves PN-22 open to be decided on its merits rather than foreclosed by a field shape.** **WHAT MUST BE WRITTEN AT THE TYPE, not only here, because this plan deletes itself:** one line on `EphemeraLudicGraphStructureNode` recording that `fromHostId` is a **shard locator, deliberately NOT the inverse of `cover`**, authority lying on the parent per the [conflict rule](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md#port-records-field-scope-and-the-conflict-rule). **Without that sentence the next reader sees a coarse field beside a fine one and tries to reconcile them** --- which happened twice in the conversation that produced this row. **Shipped in `ephemeraMeta.ts` 2026-09-17 with this row.** **[PR-C1](AGENT.presence.corpus.planning.md#pr-c1-the-contraption-in-two-rooms-and-whether-covers-nest)'s rejection of this same widening is AMENDED rather than cited:** its verdict stands and both its stated grounds do not, so this row is the replacement ground. **FALSIFIER: a read path needing the parent binding that cannot afford the one parent-host read.** | 3, 4 | **Decided 2026-09-17 --- stays coarse; a locator, not an inverse** |
| **PN-22** | **TREE or DAG --- may one presence node be named in MORE THAN ONE of its parent's covers?** **OPEN, and opened deliberately rather than answered. The author's question, 2026-09-17. TO BE COSTED, NOT ARGUED.** **The tree reading is current doctrine:** [PR-C1](AGENT.presence.corpus.planning.md#pr-c1-the-contraption-in-two-rooms-and-whether-covers-nest) Finding 1 records that `GrapplingHookGun` *"did not acquire two ports by being hosted twice --- it **inherited** them from Contraption being hosted twice. **Port arity descends**: a whole that must appear in more than one of its parent's buckets needs one port per bucket it appears in."* **That is multiplicative --- `k` alternatives at each of `h` levels yields `k^h` bindings whether or not any of them records a distinction.** **The DAG reading makes arity follow GENUINE DISTINCTION instead of ancestry:** one binding named in several parent covers wherever the interior arrangement is the same, split only where it actually differs. **The gain has the same shape as [D8](AGENT.ludicCacheRebuild.planning.md#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s 2026-09-16 amendment, one layer down** --- D8 killed a `k^h` cartesian expansion in the *support bookkeeping* by separating multiplicity-of-routes from multiplicity-of-support; this would kill `k^h` in the *binding population* by separating multiplicity-of-ancestry from multiplicity-of-arrangement. **[PN-20](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is what makes the DAG EXPRESSIBLE AT ALL:** with bare entries, *"the same binding is at both"* and *"the pairing is unstated"* render identically, so sharing could not be asserted --- and [PN-21](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) is what keeps it available. **THE QUANTIFIER IS THE DECISION, and it is easy to foreclose by accident:** [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s integrity check must read *a host **one or more** of whose covers names this presence node.* **Written as *exactly one* it silently enforces the tree.** **THE COST, accepted as real by the author: splitting becomes a RUNTIME operation.** Under the tree, arity is fixed at mint time by ancestry; under the DAG a shared binding must split the moment a distinction appears --- potentially **minting whole strings of presence nodes on the move of an ancestor.** That lands in `divide`/`merge`, which [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) still lists among *"everything on the write side of scale change"*, unsettled. ~~**HOW TO COST IT --- empirical, bounded, and on machinery that already exists: grade the corpus for whether descent-induced arity is ever REDUNDANT.**~~ **--- STRUCK 2026-09-17, same day, by the author: THE CORPUS IS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT FOR THIS QUESTION, and the error is selection bias.** The corpus is **adversarially sampled** --- its [admission rules](AGENT.presence.corpus.planning.md#phase-0-corpus) select a case for *naming the code site where it stops* or *the clause it falsifies*, i.e. for **falsifying power, never for typicality**. Grading a *frequency* question against it measures the distribution of hard cases someone chose to write down, which is the distribution of nothing. **Real usage is tables in rooms and objects in backpacks --- arity 1, where `k^h` is 1 and the DAG saves EXACTLY NOTHING.** The optimization's benefit therefore lives **only** in the multi-present regime, which is the corpus regime, where [PR-C1](AGENT.presence.corpus.planning.md#pr-c1-the-contraption-in-two-rooms-and-whether-covers-nest) already indicates the distinctions are **genuine** and there is nothing redundant to collapse either. **And no usage distribution exists to measure against: pre-rollout, single instance.** **--- AND THE ROW'S OWN PREMISE WAS WORSE THAN STATED, found the same day: THERE IS NO DESCENT AT ALL.** [`presencePortStepsForMove.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/compile/presencePortStepsForMove.ts) maps `froms` to **removes** and emits at most **one** add facing one host; nothing cascades into a moved thing's interior, and [`computeCarryClosure`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) returns a single-node graph that grows nothing (`carry` unreachable under CD3). **Depth in the presence structure comes from GRAPHS NESTING, not from any traversal** --- which is why reading one graph's local mutation says nothing about it, an error made twice in the conversation that produced this row. **[Slice 7](#recommended-order) is a REPRESENTATION MIGRATION, not new behaviour** (*"one binding per rehost, every host kind, no arity check"*), **so descent appears in NO slice of this plan** and the `k^h` cost is **latent, not being paid.** The question is how often sibling buckets of one host carry **identical** covers (pure descent overhead) versus genuinely different ones (required under either reading). **PR-C1 is already a known NEGATIVE result: GHG's buckets are `{Gun, CableSpan1}` and `{CableSpan2, Hook}` --- disjoint, so two bindings are required either way and the DAG saves nothing there.** Grade [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) (twenty appearances of one thread-span), [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) and [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) the same way. **If all four are genuine distinctions, the obligation is not worth taking on current evidence and this row closes cheap.** **Nothing shipped assumes single-parentage** --- covers do not exist in code yet, so there is no build to unwind under either answer. **If it resolves toward DAG it owes a DESIGN-tier row**, since it changes what a cover can express: PR-C1's *arity descends* would be amended to *descends only where the interior differs*, and **PR-C1's own example survives that amendment intact.** **WHERE IT LIVES NOW, and why not here: GRADUATED 2026-09-17 to [`AGENT.implementation.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.implementation.md)'s `ludicGraph/` section.** This table's own scope is *"decisions being made in order to implement the slices above"*, and this decides nothing for any slice here. **Left Open it would re-create the P7-as-a-gate shape this plan family already dismantled** --- *"a prediction made on 2026-08-21, when ports did not exist in code and presence was a word rather than a system"* --- three weeks later and in the same vocabulary. **The durable doc is the right home because this plan deletes itself and the obligation outlives it.** **Row retained rather than removed** (ids are stable and never reused) **as the forwarding address and the record of the struck method.** **Nothing is owed here; the two things that keep the choice free are already shipped** --- [PN-20](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s `{ host, presence }` entry, and [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s *one or more* quantifier. **Acknowledged openly in the same conversation: there is no settled taxonomy for shelving future-development concerns, and `AGENT.implementation.md` is the best available home rather than an obviously correct one.** | --- | **FORWARDED 2026-09-17 --- not a decision for this plan; obligation graduated to [`AGENT.implementation.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.implementation.md). Gates nothing** |
| **PN-23** | **May [Slice 7](#recommended-order)'s port retirement run BEFORE Slices 4--6, and what does the read surface get called afterwards?** **The question is the length of the bridge period**, in which a presence binding is representable both as a node and as a port. **The framing that had to be corrected first, because it drove the original answer:** Slices 4--6 look like they *wean consumers off ports onto nodes*, which would make retiring ports early a mid-flight rug-pull. **They do not.** [Slice 3](#recommended-order) already did the weaning: `nodesFromPresencePort` reads **no port record at all** --- it takes a bare uuid, prefixes it to `PRESENCE#`, and looks up a NODE. What survives into Slices 4--6 is a **naming** residue (functions and parameters still spelled *port*), not a data dependency, so the cost of retiring ports first is a **rename**, not a re-keying. **Three findings decided it, and they are written out in [Slice 7a](#recommended-order)'s first sub-bullet with their expiry conditions rather than only here** --- (i) four of five inventory items already discharged by Slice 3, (ii) no production writer mints a presence-port terminal, (iii) `fromHostId` lives on the node too. **Finding (ii) is the ordering argument and it EXPIRES:** the moment Slice 5 or 6 mints a presence-addressed terminal, retiring the port stops being free and starts being a migration. **Going first is therefore cheaper than going last by a widening margin, which is the opposite of the usual bridge-period intuition** --- and the bridge's own cost is genuinely low (a labelled tombstone type and two unchanged emitters), so the case rests on the expiry, not on discomfort at coexistence. **Second clause, and it is the one thing this row does NOT settle: where does the write side's ENUMERATED-cover obligation live?** Slice 3 mints `cover: { tag: 'Full' }` and defers real bucket-membership computation to "a later slice" **without naming one**; [Slice 5](#recommended-order)'s own bullet says the cover is written "at mint time" while its site is the cache-side fold, which reads as two different moments under one phrase. **Slice 7a does not take it** (it is a retirement, not new functionality) and Slice 7's remaining position does not silently own it either. **Whoever picks up Slice 5 must resolve this before building, and should open a row rather than absorbing it**, since the two readings put the work in different layers. | ~~7~~ 7a | ~~DECIDED 2026-09-17+ (clause 1) --- Slice 7's port retirement is pulled ahead of Slices 4--6 as [Slice 7a](#recommended-order); the `nodesFromPresencePort`/`portId` rename lands IN 7a so Slices 4--6 are written against final names.~~ **--- SHIPPED 2026-09-17+.** Clause 1 is now built, not just decided: [Slice 7a](#recommended-order) landed the rename as a full cascade (`nodesFromPresencePort` --> `nodesFromPresenceBinding`, `addPresencePort`/`removePresencePort` --> `addPresenceBinding`/`removePresenceBinding`, `presencePortStepsForMove.ts` --> `presenceBindingStepsForMove.ts`), decided in conversation with the author beyond what this row named. [PN-14](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s tombstone date and [PN-12](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s write-time pointer amended to match; neither verdict changed. ~~**Clause 2 (the enumerated-cover write obligation's home) is STILL OPEN and gates nothing until Slice 5.**~~ **--- CLAUSE 2 GRADUATED 2026-09-18, picked up with Slice 5: not a decision for this plan, and not Slice 5's to build.** Slice 5's own live-code check found the write side still mints `cover: { tag: 'Full' }` unconditionally at both emitters, with no caller needing anything narrower --- the same "gates nothing, owed by no current slice" shape as [PN-22](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only)'s graduated descent prediction, right next to it in [`AGENT.implementation.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.implementation.md)'s `ludicGraph/` section, where this obligation now lives (this plan deletes itself at Slice 8; the obligation outlives it). **Row retained as the forwarding address, per PN-22's own precedent.** |

| **PN-24** | **Does a presence binding's EXTERIOR address carry the `PRESENCE#` tag, or a bare uuid?** **DECIDED 2026-09-18 in conversation with the author: TAG IT (option B1 --- prefix the existing `.port` field, which stays typed `string`).** **The question was raised by the author against Slice 5's own account of this**, which reported the bare-uuid encoding as settled and merely stale-in-the-plan; the author asked whether there had been a *requirements* reason for it at all, and there had not been. **The reason the bare form looked principled is that it once WAS: presence was a port, so one lookup in `graph.ports` returned a record whose `kind: 'Present'` carried the discriminator, and the id needed to carry nothing.** [Slice 7a](#recommended-order) deleted that record. **Nothing inherited the discriminator** --- two disjoint collections (`presenceNodes`, `ports`) and a terminal that cannot say which one it means --- **so the convention outlived its justification, and the shipped comment defending it (*"a confirmation rather than an inference"*) was written AT that moment rather than weighed against it.** Traced to [`8b71265b4`](#recommended-order) (Slice 3), not 7a. **Two costs were already being paid:** Slice 5 item 3's site (b) could not be built at all (a *unary* key function has no second value to confirm against, so it cannot classify a bare id), and [Slice 6](#recommended-order)'s guard faced the same wall --- its two opposite verdicts (absent presence binding = **legal**; dangling crossing port = **corruption**) are indistinguishable without the tag, which is why `resolveEndpoint`'s decline message had conflated them into one string. **The deciding frame is that *confirmation* and *inference* are not alternatives: the tag says WHICH COLLECTION to read, the lookup still confirms existence.** The bare form conflated the two questions and answered both with one search, which is exactly what made a miss ambiguous. **B2 (a third terminal-union arm with its own declared field, giving compile-time discrimination) was weighed and REJECTED** --- it was smaller than it looked (only ~6 production sites dispatch on the union's shape), but not worth a third arm in a widely-consumed union for a runtime-checkable tag. **Accepted cost, named: double-prefixing stays representable at compile time**, mitigated by routing every remaining construction through `PresenceKey` (below) rather than by a type. **Cost window, and it is why this was done NOW: no production writer mints an exterior presence address** (`buildCrossingLegs.ts` always mints a fresh crossing uuid), so there was **zero stored data to migrate** --- the same expiry logic [PN-23](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) used for the port retirement, and it would have closed the moment a writer appeared. **Two things the build discovered that the decision did not anticipate, both now load-bearing: (1) `isEphemeraPresenceNodeId` THROWS on any value containing more than one `#`** (`isEphemeraTaggedId`'s deliberate *Illegal nested EphemeraId* check) **and a minted stub port id embeds component ids** (`STUB-["OBJECT#C","OBJECT#D","Under",""]`), so an EphemeraId guard on a port id is a **category error that throws on ordinary same-host traffic** --- it broke 8 merge tests before being caught. **`isPresenceTaggedPortId` ([`ephemeraMeta.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts)) is the total-over-port-ids predicate to use instead; do not "simplify" it back.** **(2) The house utility for this already existed and presence was the outlier:** [`enforceTypedKey`](../../../../../packages/mtw-utilities/ts/types.ts) (115 uses in `lambda/ephemera`) is idempotent on a correct tag and throws on a wrong one. `PresenceKey` added beside `RoomKey`/`ObjectKey`, `'PRESENCE'` added to `PrefixKey` --- **which does NOT contradict `EphemeraPresenceNodeId`'s rule that it stays out of the `EphemeraId` allowlist**, since `PrefixKey` already carries `CONNECTION`/`SESSION` and is a typed-string-key union, not the component-addressable universe. | 5 (closes item 3), unblocks 6 | **SHIPPED 2026-09-18.** Convention stated on `EphemeraLudicPortAddress`; `ephemeraLudicTerminalsEqual` de-reconstructed (it would have silently double-prefixed); `resolveEndpoint` dispatches on the tag and splits its conflated decline into two reasons; `collapsedEdgeIdentityKey` normalizes DOWN to the bare id, **closing Slice 5 item 3 site (b)**; five remaining `PRESENCE#` constructions routed through `PresenceKey`, retiring one `as EphemeraPresenceNodeId` cast. Suites green: `mtw-utilities` 147, `mtw-interfaces` 746, `lambda/ephemera` 2911, `mtw-gateways` 265, `lambda/diagnostics` 117; `tsc --noEmit` clean on all three touched packages. |

## Verification

Per slice, from the repo root:

```bash
# Slices 2--5 (types, cut, cache, merge)
cd lambda/ephemera && npm run test -- --watchAll=false dataSource/positions/

# Slice 2 also touches the shared package
cd packages/mtw-interfaces && npm run test

# Slice 7 (write side) and before any slice is called done
cd lambda/ephemera && npm run test -- --watchAll=false
```

**The full-suite run is not optional after Slices 2 and 7.** `*.integration.test.ts` files sit outside the tsconfig include and mock by module **path**, so a widened union or a moved export can typecheck clean and still break them. **Grep module paths, not only symbols**, when anything moves.

**Anchor check for Slice 0.** Cross-file anchors fail silently. After the amendment pass, confirm each new link resolves --- prefer copying an existing link to deriving a slug, since a `---` in a heading is an em dash and its anchor takes two hyphens.

**A note on green tests, because this migration is unusually exposed to it.** Untouched-branch tests staying green can mean they only ever hit a degenerate case. **The degenerate case here is real and pervasive: nothing in the shipped write side mints a multi-binding whole**, so the arity-1 path is the only one most fixtures exercise ~~--- and clauses 5 and 6 differ *only* above arity 1~~ **--- amended 2026-09-15: clauses 5 and 6 no longer differ anywhere ([PN-1](#open-decisions-implementation--plan-only) collapsed them), which makes this hazard WORSE rather than better, not moot.** The old framing let a reader infer that arity-1 fixtures were testing the clause that governs them; under one rule they are testing the *only* rule at its least demanding point, and a merge that silently kept an arity-1 shortcut would pass every one of them. **The specific regression to write a failing test for first: a `Present` link omitted at arity 1.** **Every slice that touches the cover needs a multi-binding fixture written deliberately**, and new tests should be checked against the unfixed code and seen to fail there.

## Lifecycle

**Delete this file at Slice 8**, after the graduation sweep. The corpus ([`AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md)) and the presence plan ([`AGENT.presence.planning.md`](AGENT.presence.planning.md)) are **not** this plan's to dispose --- both outlive it, and the rows Slice 0 amends there are theirs. **Anything in this file still true when it goes belongs in `AGENT.concepts.md`, `AGENT.contract.md` or `AGENT.implementation.md`, not back in a design plan.** Every `PN-` row must be closed or forwarded first; a bare marker left behind is the dangling-pointer anti-pattern, and the ids are permanent.
